# Review · 001 Period planner v1

## Pre-implementation analysis (phase 4)

First pass on 2026-11-11 at commit `ef3e852` (branch `001-planificador-v1-spec`), with a clean context. Inputs: `spec.md`, `plan.md`, `tasks.md`, ADR-004, `source-en.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/constitution.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/security.md`, `eslint.config.js`, `scripts/check-traceability.mjs`, `scripts/verify.sh`, `jest.config.js`, `stryker.config.json` and `.github/workflows/verify.yml`.

Second pass on 2026-11-11 at commit `b7b4b9a` (same branch), by a new reviewer with a clean context. Inputs: the same files, plus `package.json`. Every first-pass finding was checked against the current files, and P-21 to P-29 are new.

Third pass on 2026-11-11 at commit `2dd63d0` (same branch), a short pass by a new reviewer with a clean context. Inputs: `spec.md`, `plan.md`, `tasks.md`, ADR-004, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/constitution.md` and `docs/architecture.md`. It checks P-21 to P-29 and repeats the coverage and consistency checks; P-30 is new. `tasks.md` was renumbered (T001–T079, review P-27), so the findings of the first and second passes quote the old task numbers.

### Second pass: status of P-01 to P-20

- **P-01 Fixed.** `src/state/` publishes `MatchView` (columns, rows with counters and red flags, cells with state and light red, legend, format options, players to add, replaceable starters), `MatchListItem` with dates already formatted, and `formatOptions`. Repositories return plain records, and state narrows them with `isFormat` and sorts them with the domain comparators. Against the `boundaries` policies in `eslint.config.js`, every planned import is allowed: app and ui import state values and domain types, state imports domain and data, and data imports only domain types and the `expo-sqlite` and `drizzle-orm` subpaths.
- **P-02 Fixed.** The helper is `tests/support/test-database.ts`. It matches `TEST_FILES` (`tests/**`), it is not a `*.test.ts` file, so Jest does not run it as a suite, and it is outside `collectCoverageFrom`.
- **P-03 Fixed.** Plan decisions 1–8 and the extra task behaviours are recorded in the spec as RF-083–RF-088, E-020–E-032 and clarifications D37–D47, and the texts table includes `error.saveFailed`, the header marks and `match.clearTime`. Leftovers are in P-23, P-25 and P-26.
- **P-04 Fixed.** RF-088 and E-028 were added. The config plugin covers Android `dataExtractionRules`, with `<cloud-backup>` and `<device-transfer>`, and `fullBackupContent`, and adds `isExcludedFromBackup` on the iOS database folder. The constitution check says how, Security audits it in T023, and `docs/security.md` is updated there.
- **P-05 Fixed.** RF-082 separates grid changes and confirmed actions, saved at once, from forms saved with «Guardar». E-029 and D40 were added.
- **P-06 Fixed.** The oracle covers all three formats, with call-ups from the format minimum to 13 and at most 4 X missing. It has a mixed generator with both outcomes guaranteed, and a possible-by-construction generator. Checked by hand: a cyclic assignment gives every player ⌊T/n⌋ or ⌈T/n⌉ X, where T is 15, 25 or 30. That meets both minimums for every in-range call-up (4 periods, 8–12 players: 1–2 played, 1–2 rested; 6 periods, 9–12: 2–3 played, 2–3 rested; 8 periods, 8–12: 2–4 played, 2–4 rested). Circled X go only into cells that were empty in the valid window, so refilling the removed X always works.
- **P-07 Fixed.** The plugin was dropped in favour of `scripts/bundle-migrations.mjs`, with a staleness test.
- **P-08 Fixed.** `jest.stryker.config.js` runs only domain tests, without the budget test and with `numRuns: 100`. It also sets an explicit `timeoutMS` and records the run time at each close, with the incremental-mode fallback (T013, T021).
- **P-09 Fixed.** The plan "Pending" field and the task "Pending:" fields no longer point to pending questions. The exception is T040, see P-22.
- **P-10 Fixed.** The paragraph above the examples says which examples state the whole grid. E-001, E-002, E-005 and E-008–E-010 now fix the call-up and the rest of the grid, E-015 shows the rule-impossible warning, and E-030 was added. The exception is E-007, see P-24.
- **P-11 Fixed** for the tasks it named: the old T016, T019, T031 and T040 were split (now T018–T020, T024–T026, T031 and T040, and T051–T052). T030 is still large, see P-27.
- **P-12 Fixed.** T022 installs every D2 package, `zustand` included. T037 creates the session store before the call-up actions use it. `openCellMenu` and `matches-store.ts` are in the plan's state design. The quick home/away option is `useMyTeamAs` in the draft store, and no domain function is claimed.
- **P-13 Fixed.** US1, US2 and US6 all list RF-001. RF-074 and RF-075 are recorded through review P-13. The Q20 row says the texts are approved.
- **P-14 Fixed.** The plan uses `Team`, `Player`, `Match`, `CallUp`, `Format`, `Period`, `Overtime`, `RuleWindow` and `Mark`. Every new code name is in "Glossary additions" (T002). `Warning` appears as a suffix (`Warnings`, `PeriodWarning`, `CallUpRangeWarning`), which is acceptable.
- **P-15 Fixed.** ADR-004 now names the `NNN-name-spec` branch. T001 sets the spec to "In progress" and adds the numbering rule to `specifier.md`. The D1 scope lists T001, T002 and T013. New stale text is in P-29.
- **P-16 Accepted.** The plan's table "IDs that a machine can only check in part" accepts it explicitly and names the Jest tests and the manual checks. It becomes final when Fernando approves the plan.
- **P-17 Fixed.** The principle 1 row and the D2 checkpoint state the known D2 gap.
- **P-18 Fixed.** Rows are keyed by `CallUpEntryId`, and a re-added player gets a new entry. T014, T017, T019, T035 and T037 test it.
- **P-19 Fixed.** The plan has a height table: 238 pt above the rows and 530 pt left, which is at least 528. The banners are measured with `onLayout`, the grid buttons go in the header on tablets, and T048 and T052 check it. The margin is recorded as a risk.
- **P-20 Fixed.** The property now says "every warning it reports is justified", and there is a separate example for a complete, valid grid.

### New findings (second pass)

- **P-21 · Important.** RF-057 says the undo history "is lost when leaving [the match]". The plan empties it in `closeMatch()`, and T050 lists "leaving the screen empties the history" as a scenario. The plan never says when `closeMatch()` runs. Two flows of the spec happen on child routes of the match:
  - E-026 removes Eva from the call-up on `/matches/[matchId]/call-up` (T041), then presses Undo back on the grid;
  - RF-086 skips the cells of periods that disappeared in a format change, and that change happens on `/matches/[matchId]/edit` (T067).

  If `closeMatch()` runs when the grid screen loses focus or unmounts, which T050's wording invites, the history is empty when the coach comes back. E-026 then fails in the real app, while the state tests of T047 still pass, because they call the store directly. The plan should define "leaving the match" (for example, the match route is popped back to the team or the match list, and opening the call-up, new-player or edit routes is not leaving). T050's scenario should be reworded to match, and T051 or T043 should add a Maestro step for E-026 through the call-up screen. If the intended meaning differs, the Specifier should record it in RF-057.

- **P-22 · Minor.** The plan and the tasks do not fully carry D44–D46.
  - The `choice-dialog.tsx` design describes tapping outside, but not the Android Back button. With React Native `Modal`, that is `onRequestClose`: it must do nothing for confirmations and cancel questions, the replaced-X choice, the reminder and the menu.
  - T040 still has "Pending: Android back button behaviour (plan "Pending", point 2), to be recorded in the spec before approval", but the plan has no such point and D46 settled it.
  - The scenarios of T050, T059, T060, T067, T071 and T072 mention tapping outside but not Back.
  - T060 adds «Cancelar» to the long-press menu, while RF-052 says "the menu always shows the three options". A cancel control is reasonable, but the plan should say it is not a fourth option.
- **P-23 · Minor.** Some interface texts that the plan implies are not in the approved Spanish texts table:
  - the "not found" state of `route-params.ts` and T031 (or the plan should say it goes back silently);
  - the accessible labels of the three cell states (T049), which ESLint checks as JSX attributes;
  - the empty-state text of the "players to add" list (T041).

  Principle 12 and the Specifier's texts table cover these. Add them through the Specifier, or state that the screens show no text there.

- **P-24 · Minor.** The paragraph above the examples says E-007 states the whole grid and that anything not listed is not shown. E-007 does not fix P6, and it does not say "no subs". A circled X in P5 would turn its «!» into «≠», and a sub in P6 with fewer than 5 X would add «≠» on P6, so "Only «!» on P5" depends on how the test fills the grid. Add "no subs; P6 is empty", as E-005 does. By-hand check of E-007 otherwise: P1–P4 hold 20 X across 10 players with at most 5 per period, so each period has exactly 5 X. Every player has 3 possible rests, so no light red cell, and U = 1. With five slots in P5 and ten players able to take one, the window is feasible.
- **P-25 · Minor.** RF-081 says "the whole grid of the format fits in both orientations" on a tablet, and its criterion uses 12 players. RF-010 allows 13 or more. With 44-pt rows, the iPad landscape budget (530 pt) holds only 12. T048 decides that "13 players on a tablet → vertical scroll allowed", which the spec does not say. Record it in RF-081 through the Specifier, as was done for the overtimes in Q19.
- **P-26 · Minor.** RF-082 treats "match details" as a form saved with «Guardar», and "the format change of a saved match, once confirmed" as saved at once. The plan puts both on `/matches/[matchId]/edit`, through `match-form.tsx` and `changeFormat` in the session store. Neither the plan nor T067 says that the format picker there acts on its own (confirmation, then saved at once) and outside «Guardar». They also do not say what the coach sees after confirming a format change and then leaving without «Guardar». T067's scenario "leaving without «Guardar» keeps the saved details" could be read as also reverting the format. State it in the plan's US6 design and in T067.
- **P-27 · Minor.** These two tasks look longer than three hours.
  - T023 covers two Android XML files with manifest attributes, an iOS `AppDelegate.swift` modification with an anchor check and idempotent merge, a Jest test, `prebuild` on both platforms, device checks with `xattr` and `bmgr`, the Security audit and `docs/security.md`. Split it into Android and iOS tasks.
  - T030 covers the root layout, three routes, `team-form.tsx`, `format-picker.tsx`, `input-limits.ts`, `save-error-banner.tsx`, `es.json` and tests. Split it into the list and navigation, then the form and its edit route.

  T001, T019 and T037 are also at the edge. D2 is re-planned before it starts, so only T001 and T019 (D1) need a look now.

- **P-28 · Minor.** The plan creates folders that the "Structure" of `docs/architecture.md` does not list: `plugins/` (config plugin), `tests/support/`, `tests/scripts/`, `tests/plugins/` and `tests/setup/`. Rule 12 asks to respect the structure, and rule 11 asks for an ADR for architecture changes. Add these folders to the structure in the documentation PR, as a consequence of ADR-004 or as a recorded clarification.
- **P-29 · Minor.** In ADR-004, "the next spec continues after the highest ID used so far (today `RF-089` and `E-033`)" reads as if those were the highest IDs used. The highest are `RF-088` and `E-032`, and the next spec starts at `RF-089` and `E-033`. The discarded alternative "spec 001 already has 19 examples" should now say 32.

### Second pass: checks with no finding

- **Coverage:** every RF-001–RF-088 and every E-001–E-032 appears on at least one task checkbox line, which is where `check-traceability.mjs` reads them. Every task ID points to an existing RF and E. T001–T074 are sequential, with no gaps or repeats. No `[DOUBT]` is open (D1–D47 answered and dated). The level, Large, is right.
- **Examples checked by hand** for the new or changed ones:
  - E-001: Juan has U = 0 and the nine others have L = 2 and U = 3; 22 X are needed, between 18 and 27.
  - E-002: P3 shows «≠»; 23 X are needed.
  - E-005: 23 X fit both counts (20 + 3 and 1 + 11 × 2). Pedro has 4 possible rests, and the others have 3. Two slots are left in P5, and Pedro, with L = 1, takes one: feasible.
  - E-008: 26 X are needed, between 18 and 36.
  - E-009: P3 is light red at the minimum of 1; 13 X are needed, between 9 and 18.
  - E-010: 14 X are needed, between 9 and 18.
  - E-015: at most 8 × 3 = 24 of 25 X.
  - E-030: at least 13 × 2 = 26 of 25 X.
  - E-026: the replacement entry keeps Ana's cell after Eva is pruned, so P2 has 4 X.
  - E-031: the overtime entry is pruned on removal, and one press empties Ana's P1.

  All of them match RF-062–RF-067. E-007 also holds once P-24 is fixed.

- **Layers and conventions:** domain functions are pure and never read the clock, and "today" comes from state. `grid-layout.ts` computes only sizes. Input limits are interface constants, not regulation rules. No `react-native` is imported in `src/state/`.
- **Dependencies:** each one has its reason, alternative, licence and security review. `expo-splash-screen`, `@expo/ui` and `react-native-gesture-handler` are already in `package.json`. No new dependency makes network calls.
- **Spec, plan and tasks:** the task numbers quoted in the plan (T013, T020, T023, T024, T027, T032, T033, T038, T043, T048, T051, T052, T055, T060–T062, T068 and T074) match `tasks.md`. The budget numbers (500 calls, 2.5 s) and the mutation settings agree between the plan and T013. The D2 and D3 split agrees with ADR-004.
- **Source coverage:** every item of `source-en.md` maps to an RF, an example or "Out of scope" in the spec's coverage table. Every addition is traced to Q1–Q20, plan decisions 1–9, review findings or D36–D47. The one exception is P-25.

### New findings (third pass)

- **P-30 · Important.** The plan does not say how the new-match screen moves to the grid of the match it has just created (T044: "creating opens the match screen"). RF-057 and plan decision 12 define leaving the match as going back to the team or to a match list. Suppose `/teams/[teamId]/matches/new` pushes `/matches/[matchId]`, the natural `router.push`. Then Back from the grid shows the new-match form again, still holding its draft. That is neither the team nor a list. Pressing «Guardar» there would create a second match with the same call-up. T040 only drops the draft "when the screen closes without «Guardar»", and here the screen never closed.
  - **Fix:** in the plan's navigation section and in T044, state that a successful «Guardar» replaces the new-match route with the grid (`router.replace`, or `dismiss` followed by `push`, as the SDK 58 Expo Router docs advise). Back from the new match then goes to the team screen.
  - **Tests:** T044 should have a `renderRouter` scenario, "create, then Back → team screen, and the match is listed once". T048 should include that step in Maestro.

### First pass details

- **P-01 · Important.** `docs/architecture.md` and `eslint.config.js` let `src/app/`, `src/ui/` and `src/data/` import only types from `src/domain/`. The plan exposes from `match-session-store.ts` only `grid` and `warnings`, so the grid components (T038, T041) would need `gridColumns`, `columnKey`, `cellKey` (to look up `GridWarnings.lightRedCells`), `cellState`, `playerCounter` and `isRuleWindowColumn`, and `format-picker.tsx` (T023) would need `FORMAT_IDS`. On the data side, T034 asks `loadGrid` to return the call-up "sorted", which needs `comparePlayers`, and turning the stored integer into `FormatId` needs `isFormatId`. All of these are domain functions, so ESLint `boundaries` would fail. The plan must define what state publishes for the interface (for example a grid view with columns, rows with counters, cell states and warning flags, and the format options), and move sorting and narrowing from `src/data/` to `src/state/`.
- **P-02 · Important.** T019 puts `src/data/test-database.ts`, a helper built on `better-sqlite3`, inside `src/data/`. It is not a `*.test.ts` file, so the boundaries rule for `data` (only `expo-sqlite` and `drizzle-orm`) rejects the import. This contradicts the plan's own line "`better-sqlite3` is only imported by tests", and the helper would also count in coverage. Move it to `tests/` (test category) or name it so that it falls under `TEST_FILES`.
- **P-03 · Important.** The nine plan questions were answered, but the answers live only in `plan.md`. Several of them are user-visible behaviour that the spec does not contain: "07" rejected (RF-016 says "one or two digits"), length limits of 40 and 500 and scores 0–999 (RF-013, RF-016, RF-025), the date shown as «11/11/2026», the format being editable in the new-match form, the save-failure message and its new key `error.saveFailed` (not in the approved Spanish texts table), undo skipping missing cells (RF-057), and the legend with «≠» and «!» (RF-065, RF-066). Constitution principle 12 says the answer is recorded in the spec, and the Planner role forbids adding scope that is not in the spec. Some tasks also add small behaviour of their own: T029 "draft discarded when leaving", T046 "tapping outside the dialog cancels it", T051 "change to the same format still asks". Record them through the Specifier (Clarifications, the affected RFs and the texts table) before approval.
- **P-04 · Important.** Principle 5 says children's names never leave the device. By default, iOS iCloud and computer backups include the app's data, and the plan leaves this to Security in T018 (question 6) while the constitution check marks principle 5 "Yes" and "Pending: none". Mark the row "Pending", and settle it before D2 approval, because D2 is the first delivery that stores names. Security should also check whether `android.allowBackup: false` still allows device-to-device transfer on Android 12 and later, which may need `dataExtractionRules`. If the decision adds native configuration, `docs/security.md` should record it.
- **P-05 · Important.** RF-082 says "Every change is saved on the device at once, without a save button". The approved Spanish texts include `common.save` «Guardar» "in forms", and the plan has team, player and match forms. The spec should say what RF-082 covers: grid marks and confirmed actions are saved at once, while forms are saved with «Guardar». It should also say what happens to a half-filled form when the app closes, because E-019 and the T022, T029 and T035 "Interruptions" scenarios depend on it.
- **P-06 · Important.** Plan and T016 compare `isRuleImpossible` with brute force "up to 5 players × 3 window periods". Three window periods means the 4-period format, where every window period needs 5 X and every player needs one rest. With 5 players or fewer, no filling can give 5 X per period and a rest to everyone, so the rule is always impossible and both sides always return `true`: the property proves nothing. Generate call-ups of at least 8 players (4 periods) and 9 to 12 players (6 and 8 periods), with most cells already filled, so that brute force runs only over a few empty cells. Cover all three formats, because the minimums of 2 are never exercised otherwise.
- **P-07 · Important.** `docs/security.md` requires every new dependency to be maintained. `babel-plugin-inline-import` was last released in 2018, which the plan itself records. Either take the plan's own no-dependency fallback (a script that turns the generated `.sql` files into TypeScript modules), or record an explicit exception that Fernando approves.
- **P-08 · Important.** With `coverageAnalysis: "perTest"`, Stryker reruns, for every covering mutant, the fast-check properties (5 × 1,000 runs, one with a brute-force oracle) and the 1,000-call budget test of T012. Mutation testing also runs in D2 and D3, because the domain changes there, and the CI job has `timeout-minutes: 30`. The risk table does not cover this. The 2-second budget test is also timing-sensitive under coverage instrumentation. Plan a mitigation, for example fewer runs under Stryker through an environment variable, keeping the budget test out of mutation runs, or a separate timeout.
- **P-09 · Minor.** "Pending: none … they are decisions now" contradicts the rest of the plan and tasks. Constitution rows 5 and 12 still say "pending question" ("a proposal, not a decision"), the signatures carry "(pending question 1)", and the "Pending:" fields of T003, T018, T020, T026, T029, T030, T033, T035, T036 and T041 still point to "pending question N". Reword them to "decision N".
- **P-10 · Minor.** E-007 is fully determined, and E-003, E-004 and E-006 hold whatever the rest of the grid is. E-001, E-002, E-005 and E-008–E-010 do not fix the call-up or the other players' marks, so whether the rule-impossible warning (RF-067) and the incomplete marks appear depends on how the test fills the grid. E-003 and E-004 now list the general warning and E-005 does not, so readers may assume it is absent. A consequence worth telling Fernando: a call-up below the minimum always makes the rule impossible (8 players in 6 periods can give at most 24 of the 25 X), and so do 13 players in 6 periods (26 played needed, 25 X available). E-015 therefore also shows the general warning. The spec should say that each example asserts only the warnings it names, or give full grids.
- **P-11 · Minor.** These tasks look longer than three hours: T016 (five properties, the oracle and the 18-row examples table), T019 (schema, generated migration, database opening, test driver, start-up state and splash), T030 (screen plus four components), T031 (three routes plus `choice-dialog.tsx`) and T040 (two flows on two platforms plus a device check on three devices in both orientations). Split them. D2 and D3 are re-planned anyway, but T016 is in D1.
- **P-12 · Minor.** T018 installs every D2 package, but T022 says "install `zustand`". T029 adds call-up actions to `match-session-store.ts` before T035 creates that store. T045's `openCellMenu` and T051's `src/state/matches-store.ts` are not in the plan's state design. T026 says the quick home/away option is a domain function, but `match-input.ts` has no signature for it.
- **P-13 · Minor.** RF-001 lists US2 among its stories, but the US2 requirements list does not include RF-001. RF-074 shows the format and the category, which go beyond the Q17 answer ("date, time, home, away, score and team"). The Q20 row still says "(see Spanish texts, pending approval)", while that section is approved.
- **P-14 · Minor.** The glossary maps Player, Team, Match, CallUp, Format, Period, Overtime, RuleWindow and Warning to code names. The plan uses `SquadPlayer`, `ShirtNumber`, `FormatId` and `FormatConfig`, `GridWarnings` and `Column` kinds instead, and does not define `Player`, `Team`, `Match`, `RuleWindow` or `Warning`. Align the names, or add the new ones to the T002 glossary table (principle 17, rule 12).
- **P-15 · Minor.** In ADR-004: the documentation branch `001-planificador-v1-spec` fits neither the current rule (`001-planificador-v1`) nor the new one (`NNN-name-dN`). No task sets the spec to "In progress" when D1 starts. D1 is described as "only `src/domain/` plus the traceability script and its documentation", but T002 also edits the glossary in `docs/architecture.md`. The consequence "the Specifier must look up the highest `RF` and `E`" is not among T001's changes to `specifier.md`.
- **P-16 · Minor.** The traceability script counts any YAML name under `e2e/`, but Maestro does not run in CI. Requirements whose only realistic test is a manual Maestro flow or a device check (RF-011 offline, RF-076 and RF-080–RF-081 on devices) pass the machine check without being executed by a machine. Accept this explicitly, or name the Jest tests that carry those IDs (for example `grid-layout.ts` for RF-078–RF-081).
- **P-17 · Minor.** In D2, a tap on an empty cell of a full rule-window period, or on an X in a period with a sub, does nothing until D3. It is stated in "Deliveries", but the principle 1 row of the constitution check says "Yes" without mentioning it, and D2's checkpoint is a real match.
- **P-18 · Minor.** Undo entries are keyed by `PlayerId`. A player removed from the call-up and added again (T028 "add her again → empty row") gets a new call-up entry with the same `PlayerId`, so an older undo entry could restore marks onto the new row, which decision 7 does not contemplate. Add the scenario to T033 or T036.
- **P-19 · Minor.** RF-081 needs 12 rows without vertical scroll on a tablet in landscape (768 pt high). The grid shares that height with the header row, the general warning, the call-up banner, the legend and the undo and overtime buttons. T037 should include them in the height calculation.
- **P-20 · Minor.** The property "`evaluateWarnings` … white on an empty, complete and valid grid" contradicts itself: an empty grid shows incomplete marks in the window. Say "a complete grid that meets every rule".

### First pass: checks with no finding

- **Coverage:** every RF-001–RF-082 and E-001–E-019 is in at least one task line; every task ID points to an existing RF and E; T001–T059 are sequential; no `[DOUBT]` is open; the level, Large, is right.
- **Source coverage:** every item of `source-en.md` maps to an RF or to Out of scope. Apart from P-03 and P-13, every addition is backed by Q1–Q20 or D36.
- **Examples checked by hand** against RF-062, RF-063 and RF-067:
  - E-003 (one possible rest: red, P5 light red, U < L, impossible);
  - E-004 (window complete, Pedro L = 1 with no X missing: impossible, red by RF-064);
  - E-006 (one X missing, two players with L = 1: impossible);
  - E-007 (ten players with L = 0 and U = 1, five X missing in P5: possible, no light red because three possible rests is more than two).

  E-001, E-002 and E-008–E-010 also match RF-062 with "at or below".

- **RF-067 algorithm:** the bounded-flow reduction in the plan is sound. Its parts are source → player [L, U], player → period with capacity 1 on empty cells, period → sink [d, d], the return edge, a super-source and super-sink, and early rejection when U < L. "Player in red implies rule impossible" holds under this definition, as RF-067 requires.
- **Deliveries:** D1 contains only `src/domain/`, the traceability script and its documentation (see P-15). D2 and D3 split cleanly by story. Each delivery's PR stays green with the "ticked tasks only" rule.
- **Dependencies:** each one is listed with its reason, its alternative, its licence and its security review (see P-07).

## Security (phase 8)

| #      | Finding                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Severity       | Status                                                                                       |
| ------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SEC-01 | Stryker incremental cache, audited at commit `2982d24` (`.github/workflows/verify.yml`, `scripts/verify.sh`). Cache poisoning from a pull request (branch or fork) into `main`: not possible. A `pull_request` run saves its cache under its own ref (`refs/pull/N/merge`), which no other ref can restore. `main` only restores caches created on `main`. Pull requests can read `main`'s cache, never the other way round. `main` also runs with `--force`, so even a bad file would be ignored there and rebuilt. No `pull_request_target` or `workflow_run` is used, which would break this isolation.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | None (checked) | No action                                                                                    |
| SEC-02 | The incremental file is trusted for the mutants it covers, and on a pull request a stale result can hide a regression. Stryker decides whether to reuse a result by comparing the mutated source file and the test files. It does not see changes in files they depend on but that are neither: `tests/support/`, `jest.stryker.config.js`, `stryker.config.json`, `package-lock.json` (Jest, TypeScript or Stryker upgrades). A mutant that was Killed stays Killed even if a changed helper weakened the tests, so a pull request can pass while a mutant would now survive. The full run on `main` (`STRYKER_FORCE=1`) catches it, but only after the merge. A poisoned file, which only the author of that same pull request can create, has the same effect, and that author can already edit the code and the workflow. The file is plain JSON read by Stryker: it executes nothing. It holds source snippets and test names of a public repository, no secrets. Fix: put a hash of those inputs in the cache key, for example `key: stryker-${{ runner.os }}-${{ hashFiles('tests/support/**', 'jest*.config.js', 'stryker.config.json', 'package-lock.json') }}-${{ github.sha }}` and the same prefix without the SHA in `restore-keys`, so a change in any of them starts from a full run. | Minor          | Fixed in D1 (cache key hashes the inputs Stryker does not track)                             |
| SEC-03 | Pinning. `actions/cache/restore@v6` and `actions/cache/save@v6` use the major tag, like `actions/checkout@v7` and `actions/setup-node@v7`: consistent with the workflow, and all three are published by GitHub (`actions/*`), so the risk is low. The tag exists (`v6`, latest release `v6.1.0`, tag commit `55cc8345863c7cc4c66a329aec7e433d2d1c52a9`). It still does not meet `docs/security.md` ("pinned versions of actions"): a major tag is mutable. There is also no `.github/dependabot.yml` to keep SHAs updated. Fix: pin the four `actions/*` to the full commit SHA with the version in a comment, and add Dependabot for the `github-actions` ecosystem. Not caused by this commit, so it can be done once for the whole workflow.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Minor          | Fixed before D2 (the four `actions/*` pinned to a full SHA, Dependabot for `github-actions`) |
| SEC-04 | Permissions: `permissions: contents: read` is enough. The cache actions talk to the cache service with the runner's own runtime token, not with `GITHUB_TOKEN`, so they need no `actions: write` (only deleting caches through the API does). On a pull request from a fork the token is read-only and the cache still works, in the scope of that pull request. Nothing to change. Do not widen the permissions.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | None (checked) | No action                                                                                    |
| SEC-05 | Script injection: none. `STRYKER_FORCE` is built from `github.ref` in a comparison and receives only the literal `1` or `0`; the key uses `runner.os` and `github.sha`, which an attacker does not control; no `${{ }}` expression is inside a `run:` script. In `verify.sh` the variables are only compared with `"1"`, and the array expansion is quoted (`${stryker_args[@]+"${stryker_args[@]}"}`, safe with `set -u`). `github.ref` is never `refs/heads/main` on a `pull_request` event, so the forced run applies only to the push to `main`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | None (checked) | No action                                                                                    |
| SEC-06 | `if: always()` on the save step is safe: it can only write `reports/stryker-incremental.json` under the key of the current commit, in the scope of the current ref, and a failed or missing file only gives a warning ("Path Validation Error"), not a job failure. Two points of hygiene, not security: (a) `always()` also runs when the run is cancelled (the concurrency group cancels older runs), and when `verify.sh` fails before Stryker it saves again the same file it restored under a new key, one entry per commit; (b) the caches are removed after 7 days without use or when the 10 GB of the repository are full, so a quiet week, or a fork pull request filling the cache (it can edit the workflow), leaves the next run without a cache: a full run of about 25 min against `timeout-minutes: 30` (measured 25 min 5 s on run 43177620495). Fix: use `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}` and raise `timeout-minutes` to 40 so a cache miss does not fail the job.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Minor          | Fixed in D1 (`!cancelled()`, timeout 40 min)                                                 |

### 1. IDs: coverage and consistency

- **Every RF and E of US1–US4 appears in a D2 task, with the exceptions in D2-A-09** (all of them are covered by the D1 domain tasks). US1 (RF-001, 011–014, 016, 017, 069, 073, 082, 085, 088), US2 (RF-001, 005, 010–012, 025–027, 030–037, 068, 082–085) and US3 (RF-042–044, 049, 055–059, 070, 071, 076–081, 086) are all listed in T025–T057. US4 is covered by T058–T060, with RF-002–RF-004, RF-007–RF-009 left to D1.
- **No task lists an ID that the spec does not define.** Highest IDs used: RF-088 and E-032.
- **The moved IDs are consistent.** E-026: moved from T052 (D2 header has no E-026, `tasks.md:706`) to T062 (`:817`, `:825`) and T066 (`:869`, `:871`); `plan.md` Deviations (`:888`), the "Leaving the match" tests (`:651`) and the constitution text agree. E-027: T058 and T059 keep it as a state and component example with `MatchView` fixtures (valid, and the traceability script reads it from their titles); only the Maestro journey moved to T066 (`:872`) and T060 says so (`:802`). Only stale mention: D2-A-12.
- The older D1 tasks still tick E-026 and E-027 (T019, T013), which is correct: they are domain tests.

### 2. "Done when" against the plan (lesson D1-01)

Cross-checked every file, function, type, component, action and text key. Matches: T030, T032 (`SaveResult`, `route-params.ts`, `format-options.ts`), T037, T038 (`loadGridRecords` moved), T039, T040 (every draft action), T042 (both `addToCallUp`: repository and domain), T045 (two closing modes), T046, T047, T049, T050, T051, T052, T058, T059. The text keys of every task exist in the spec table (`spec.md:495-585`), including the P-23 keys. Gaps: D2-A-02, D2-A-04, D2-A-08, D2-A-13, D2-A-14. Also: T024 lists `eslint.config.js` in its files, but its "Done when" does not state the change (ignore `src/data/migrations/`); `.prettierignore` does not exist today and T024 creates it (Prettier already reads `.gitignore`).

### 3. Test titles and traceability

`check-traceability.mjs` reads the literal first argument of `it`/`test` (and `it.each(...)(...)` with at most one level of nested parentheses in the first call), comments stripped, and all non-`#` text of `*.yaml` (`scripts/check-traceability.mjs:47-61`). Rule 2 of the D2 section (`tasks.md:327`) matches that exactly, including "tagged-template tables are not read". Checked against the tasks: T030, T037, T053 and T058 follow it; no task asks for IDs in `describe`, `%s` or rows. Only open points: D2-A-05 (Maestro `name`) and D2-A-17. Also check the helper `it.each(CASES)` is not written as `it.each(CASES.map(...))` (two nested levels are silently missed); rule 2 says so.

### 4. T024: the focused-test check

Pattern (`tasks.md:340`): `^[[:space:]]*((it|test|describe)(\.[A-Za-z]+)*\.(only|skip)|x(it|test|describe)|f(it|test|describe))(\.[A-Za-z]+)*[[:space:]]*[(`]`. Read against `scripts/verify.sh:46-47`, mentally tested:

| Input (at the start of a line)                  | Result   | Right?                                                      |
| ----------------------------------------------- | -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| `it("RF-079: 4 and 6 periods fit at 375 pt", …` | no match | yes: `f(it…)` needs the line to begin with `fit`            |
| a title alone on its own line, `"… fit …",`     | no match | yes: the line begins with a quote                           |
| `it.only(`                                      | match    | yes                                                         |
| `fit(`, `fdescribe(`, `ftest(`, `xit(`          | match    | yes                                                         |
| `describe.skip.each(`                           | match    | yes (`.skip` found after backtracking, `.each` by the tail) |
| `it.only.each(TABLE)(`, `test.concurrent.only(` | match    | yes                                                         |
| `it.each([...])("...")`, `it.each(TABLE)(`      | no match | yes: not focused                                            |
| `// it.only(`, `const fit =`, ` * it.only(`     | no match | yes                                                         |

No false positive or negative on the five cases requested. Remaining weaknesses, all small:

- **False negative, not at the start of a line:** `const d = process.env.CI ? describe : describe.skip;` and `if (x) it.only(...)`. The old pattern caught them. Prettier keeps normal test calls on their own line, so this only matters for conditional-skip idioms. Minor: add `describe\.skip|it\.skip|test\.skip|\.only\b` as a second, line-anywhere pattern restricted to `\.(only|skip)\b` outside quotes, or accept and document.
- **False negative with generics:** `it.only<Foo>(`: the next character must be `(` or a backtick. Rare.
- **False positive:** a helper called `fit(` at the start of a line, for example inside a multi-line `expect(\n  fit(375, 6),\n)` in the T053 tests. Plausible, because the layout file is about "fit". Avoid by naming the helper differently, and say so in T053.
- **Exit codes:** `grep` exits 2 on a missing directory; `if grep …; then` treats that as "no match", so a typo in an argument passes silently. The old script had the same `2>/dev/null`. Make the script exit 2 itself when an argument is not a directory (the T024 scenario "an empty folder → exit 0" stays true).
- **Consistency with `verify.sh` and the tests:** the same `src tests` default, the same message and `*.test.ts(x)` globs: consistent. The script needs the executable bit (`git update-index --chmod=+x`) and should `cd` to the repository root when no argument is given, as `verify.sh` does. The test must follow `tests/scripts/check-traceability.test.ts` (`spawnSync`, fixtures in a temporary folder created at run time, `IT` placeholder idea): T024's rule "quoted strings, never a template literal whose lines start with a call" is the same precaution, and it also protects the new check from flagging its own test. BSD (macOS) and GNU grep both accept this ERE; `[(`]` is fine inside single quotes.

### 5. Order, dependencies, size

- **Order is sound.** T027 creates `drizzle.config.ts` (moved from T024) with the schema, so `drizzle-kit check` in `verify.sh:58-60` never runs without one. T038 creates `loadGridRecords` for T041; T049 adds the writes. T029 (database getter, `jest.mock` helper) comes after T028 (`test-database.ts`). T033 links to routes created in T034/T035: fine at run time (see D2-A-10 for types). T040 uses `callUpEntryFor`, `initialCallUp`, `newMatchDefaults` (T037) and `createMatch` (T038). T042 uses T039. T044 uses T040 and T045 is before T046. T058 builds on T050.
- **Only forward reference found:** D2-A-02 (the shared database type). `app-stores.ts`: D2-A-13.
- **Size:** D2-A-06 (time), D2-A-07 (250 lines in `tests/`), D2-A-11 (`match-view.ts`). Every other file named in the tasks is planned under 250 lines, with splits.
- Local mutation testing (`verify.sh:66`) runs from T030 on, because the branch differs from `main` in `src/domain/` (`domain_changed`): see doubt 2.

### 6. Dependencies, layers and the constitution

- **Rule 10:** every new package has a reason, an alternative, a licence and a Security review column (`plan.md:761-771`); `better-sqlite3` and `@types/better-sqlite3` are development only, imported only from `tests/support/test-database.ts`; the Babel plugin was dropped. `@expo/ui`, `expo-splash-screen` and gesture-handler are already installed. The `osv-scanner` exception path for `drizzle-kit` needs Fernando's approval (good). Gap: D2-A-15 (stack table).
- **Layers, from the planned files:** `src/state/` imports domain and data, never `react-native` (zustand only; the clock is passed in); `src/ui/` imports state, i18n and domain types only (`grid-layout.ts` computes sizes, not rules; `choice-dialog.tsx` uses React Native `Modal`, allowed in `ui`); `src/app/` imports no data or database (`_layout.tsx` calls `initializeDatabase()` from state); `src/data/` imports only `expo-sqlite`, `drizzle-orm` and domain types (the migration `bundle.ts` is generated and ignored by lint). Tests may import everything (`eslint.config.js:58-68`), so `better-sqlite3` in `tests/support/` passes. No violation found. One reminder for T031: the common database type must be importable by `src/state/` (state may import data) without `react-native`.
- **Constitution 1–5:** (1) the 5-X limit is the only refusal, call-up and rule warnings never disable «Guardar» (T044), and the D2 gap is stated in the plan (D2-A-16); (2) marks are only deleted after a confirmation that closes only with its buttons (T045, T046, T055), `pruneHistory` never writes marks; (3) no network call, ESLint forbids it, `RF-011` is checked in T079; (4) every action saves before it publishes, with a failure path (T049, T051, T052, E-025), forms use «Guardar»; (5) no `console` in `src/`, the data never reaches a log, `allowBackup: false`, plus the config plugin for Android and iOS in T025 and T026 with a Security audit. All covered.

### 7. The Planner's open doubts

1. **Reopening a saved match in D2. Recommendation: A, in a carved-out form.** Reasons: the spec asks for it (E-019 says the match "has the same marks", US3 criterion 7 speaks about going back "to the team or to a match list" and opening it again, the US3 checkpoint says Fernando "finds everything as it was"); the plan's own navigation already assumes the grid is pushed "from the team screen or a match list" (`plan.md:648`); and D2's goal is a match Fernando can plan on a real device (`plan.md:11`, `:90`). Without a list, one Back press after «Guardar» loses the match for good, which Fernando would see as a data-loss bug even though the rows are in SQLite. B (deep link) only helps Maestro, hides the gap from users and does not let the coach recover a match; it would also leave E-019 and RF-057 unproven in the app. How to do A without breaking the traceability rule (a task is ticked only when all its IDs have tests): do **not** split T067–T071 across deliveries. Add new D2 tasks with their own IDs (renumbering the later ones as in the 2026-11-11 renumbering, or inserting before T055), each with only the IDs it delivers: (a) domain `compareMatches` (T067 moved whole: RF-074 and RF-075 are both proven by its tests; RF-074's list of every match remains a D3 UI task), (b) data `listTeamMatches` and `MatchListRecord`, (c) the team list of `matches-store.ts` (with `displayDate` and `displayTime`, E-023), (d) the matches section of the team screen with a `router.push` to `/matches/[matchId]` (`team.matches`, `matches.empty`). Then update T044 (`:613`), T048 (`:660`), T055 and T056 (remove the `[DOUBT]`s, reopen through the list instead of `openLink`), the US3 checkpoint and T071/T073 (they keep RF-074 and the all-matches list). This adds about four small tasks to D2 and no spec change; it is a plan deviation for Fernando to approve, and ADR-004's delivery table (US1–US4 for D2) needs a one-line note.
2. **Local Stryker time per task.** From T030 on every `verify.sh` runs the full mutation run (about 5 min 10 s, `review.md:198`), for about 30 tasks, most of which do not touch `src/domain/`. Recommendation: use the incremental mode locally too, already supported by `STRYKER_INCREMENTAL=1` (no script change; the second run took 39 s), and run the full local run (`STRYKER_FORCE=1`) in T030, T037 and T061, and whenever `tests/support/`, `jest*.config.js` or `stryker.config.json` change, because Stryker does not see changes in those helper files (SEC-02). Record it in `plan.md` "Deviations" with Fernando's approval, since the 2026-11-12 deviation said "local runs stay full".
3. **CI cache key hashing `tests/support/**`.** Keep it. The key is needed because Stryker does not track `tests/support/` (SEC-02); the domain tests use `grid-*.ts`, `action-interpreter.ts` and `feasibility-oracle.ts` from there. The cost is that T028 (`test-database.ts`) and T029 (the `jest.mock` helper) force one full run (about 25 min, under the 40 min timeout) that is unrelated to the domain. It is small in practice: `package-lock.json` already changes in T024, so the first D2 run is full anyway, and a PR is normally pushed once or a few times. Prefer to land all D2 changes to `tests/support/` before the first push; if the cost ever matters, move the database helper to a folder outside the hash (and add it to the structure list of `docs/architecture.md` through ADR-004's consequences), not narrow the glob.
4. (From `plan.md:39`) The `osv-scanner` exception for `drizzle-kit` is conditional and correctly left to Fernando; no action now.

### 8. Prettier

I could not run Prettier. Reading the files, the tables of `plan.md` (constitution check, deliveries, data, dependencies, risks, deviations) and of this section keep every column at one width, and the nested code block in T024 sits at the list indentation Prettier uses; the Planner reports that `prettier --write` was run afterwards, which fits what I see. The long rows of the table above were written in the same style; `npm run verify` (Format step) is the authority.

### Verdict

- **Blocking:** one (D2-A-01): the open `[DOUBT]` about reopening a saved match. Fernando must choose before the D2 approval; recommendation A in the carved-out form above.
- **Important:** seven (D2-A-02 to D2-A-08). None needs a change of the spec or the constitution. D2-A-07 needs a task (and no ADR).
- **Minor:** nine (D2-A-09 to D2-A-17), plus the small hardening points of the T024 regex.
- The spec, plan and tasks are otherwise consistent: IDs, moved examples (E-026, E-027), names, text keys, layer rules, dependency reasons and constitution principles 1–5 check out. After the Planner resolves D2-A-01 to D2-A-08 and Fernando answers the doubts, phase 4 for D2 can close. No change level reclassification is needed (Large, with `src/domain/` touched only in T030 and T037).

## Security · T024 (D2, 2026-11-13)

Audit of the uncommitted T024 working tree on branch `001-planificador-v1-d2` (base `ab065a5`): `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `app.json`, `eslint.config.js`, `.prettierignore`, `osv-scanner.toml` and the installed packages in `node_modules/`. Method: `docs/security.md` "Audit of each change", the lockfile, the package sources, `npm ls`, the GitHub advisory and the three scanners of `scripts/verify.sh`.

### Dependencies

Its `netlify-db` and `planetscale-serverless` drivers use `fetch`, but they are separate subpaths that are never imported |
| `zustand`               | 5.1.0   | app  | MIT        | Modified 2026-11-06                       | None                    | No                                                                            | None. The `devtools` middleware talks to the Redux DevTools browser extension: do not use it                                                                 |
| `drizzle-kit`           | 0.31.11 | dev  | MIT        | Modified 2026-11-06                       | None                    | No (bundles esbuild binaries)                                                 | Dev tool only; no telemetry found in `bin.cjs`; its database drivers are never run by `db:generate` / `check`                                                |
| `better-sqlite3`        | 13.1.1  | dev  | MIT        | Modified 2026-11-11, `engines node >= 22` | None (`gypfile: false`) | Yes: prebuilt `.node` files shipped inside the package (darwin, linux, win32) | None. It downloads nothing at install, so no binary is fetched from outside the registry                                                                     |

Transitive additions: 89 lockfile entries added, none removed, no version of an existing package changed. The new ones are `await-lock`, `@drizzle-team/brocli`, `tsx`, `node-addon-api` (8.9.2, nested in `better-sqlite3`), `@esbuild-kit/*` and `esbuild` binaries (0.18.20, 0.25.12, 0.28.2), all dev except `await-lock`. The large line churn of `package-lock.json` (about 4,800 lines) is the new `license` fields and flag changes npm wrote, not version changes. The only `hasInstallScript` entries are `@parcel/watcher`, `fsevents` and the three `esbuild` copies, which are the same kinds as before T024 (esbuild is new, dev only, and is the usual platform-binary check, no download).

### Checks of the task

- **`better-sqlite3` cannot reach the app bundle.** It is in `devDependencies`. The lockfile marks it `devOptional` (an optional peer of `drizzle-orm`), which npm uses for a package needed by dev tools and only optional for production. `npm ls esbuild --omit=dev` and `npm ls drizzle-kit --omit=dev` are empty. Nothing in `src/`, `app.json` or `scripts/` imports it today, and the plan allows it only from `tests/support/test-database.ts`. Metro bundles only what is imported, and `eslint.config.js` lets `src/data` import only `expo-sqlite` and `drizzle-orm`.
- **No new permission, no plist key.** The `expo-sqlite` plugin (`plugin/build/withSQLite.js`) only writes Gradle and Podfile properties (`expo.sqlite.*`), and only when options are passed; `app.json` passes none, so it writes nothing. `node_modules/expo-sqlite/android/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` is an empty `<manifest>`. No `ios/` or `android/` folder exists in the repository. The `INTERNET` permission already in the generated manifest comes from the template and the dev client before T024 (the Implementer's before and after lists are identical); it is handled by the release checklist ("Block the Internet permission in the Android production build").
- **`app.json`** still has `android.allowBackup: false` and no new scheme or permission. The only change is the `expo-sqlite` plugin entry.
- **Children's data (principle 5).** None of the packages sends data out. `expo-sqlite` stores in the app's private storage, and the backup config plugin planned in the plan decides what leaves in backups (T025/T026, audited there).
- **Diff hygiene.** `gitleaks` finds no secrets (history and working tree). The diff holds no unexpected files: `.prettierignore` and the `src/data/migrations/` ESLint ignore only cover the generated migrations (T027), which are not hand-written.

### The esbuild exception (GHSA-67mh-4wv8-2f99)

Verified, not only copied from the request:

- `npm ls esbuild` shows `esbuild@0.18.20` only under `drizzle-kit > @esbuild-kit/esm-loader > @esbuild-kit/core-utils`. `drizzle-kit` itself and `tsx` use 0.25.12 and 0.28.2, which are above the first patched version (0.25.0).
- The advisory (medium, CVSS 5.3, `AV:N/AC:H/UI:R`, affects esbuild `<= 0.24.2`) is about the CORS headers of esbuild's own development server, `serve`. `@esbuild-kit/*` does not call `serve` (no match in its sources), and it only compiles files for the Node loader.
- It is a dev tool: `npm ls ... --omit=dev` is empty, so it never reaches the app bundle. `@esbuild-kit/esm-loader` is deprecated on npm ("Merged into tsx"), and `drizzle-kit` still pins it, so there is no fixed version we can install.

Entry added to `osv-scanner.toml` (expiry 2027-02-10, 89 days; Fernando approved it on 2026-11-13 in the chat). The file header now also names 2026-11-13 and `drizzle-kit`.

| SEC-T024-2 | Minor    | `better-sqlite3` is not imported anywhere yet. When T028 creates `tests/support/test-database.ts`, check that it is the only importer (`grep -rn "better-sqlite3" src app` must stay empty) and that `drizzle-orm` is imported only through the `drizzle-orm/expo-sqlite` and `drizzle-orm/better-sqlite3` subpaths, never from the package root, so no unused driver code is bundled. | Open, for T028                                      |
| SEC-T024-3 | Minor    | `zustand`'s `devtools` middleware must not be used in the stores: it would push the state, with children's names, to the Redux DevTools extension in dev builds. `persist` is not wanted either (the database is the only storage).                                                                                                                                                    | Open, for T037 and T041; the Codex review can check |
| SEC-T024-4 | Minor    | The exception for `esbuild` 0.18.20 expires on 2027-02-10, like the nine existing ones (2027-02-08): renew or remove all of them together. It can be dropped earlier if `drizzle-kit` stops depending on `@esbuild-kit/esm-loader`.                                                                                                                                                    | Open                                                |

No blocking and no important finding. T024 meets the Security gate: every package has a reason in `plan.md`, a compatible licence, active maintenance, no install script that downloads code, no network use in the app, and no new permission.

## Security · T026 (D2, 2026-11-13)

Audit of the whole `plugins/with-backup-exclusion.js` (Android part, committed in T025 as `0eb41ce`, and iOS part, uncommitted) against constitution principles 3 and 5, RF-088 and E-028. Method: reading the plugin, its tests and `app.json`; `npx expo prebuild --no-install --clean` run in a temporary copy outside the repository (no `ios/` or `android/` in the repository, nothing committed); inspection of the generated files; `swiftc -parse` of the generated `AppDelegate.swift`; a small standalone Swift program that runs the block on macOS; `npx jest tests/plugins` (52 passed); `gitleaks dir` (no leaks).

### Android (prebuilt output)

- `AndroidManifest.xml` has `android:allowBackup="false"`, `android:dataExtractionRules="@xml/data_extraction_rules"` and `android:fullBackupContent="@xml/full_backup_content"` once each on `<application>`. `app.json` has `android.allowBackup: false` and lists the plugin once.
- `data_extraction_rules.xml` has `<cloud-backup>` and `<device-transfer>`, each with the nine domains (`root`, `file`, `database`, `sharedpref`, `external`, `device_root`, `device_file`, `device_database`, `device_sharedpref`). `full_backup_content.xml` has the same nine. Files are overwritten, so a second prebuild cannot leave a stale rule.
- No permission was added by the plugin. The `INTERNET` permission comes from the template and the dev client and stays on the release checklist.

### iOS (prebuilt output)

- **Placement.** The tagged block `with-backup-exclusion-ios` appears once, inside `application(_:didFinishLaunchingWithOptions:)`, before `let delegate = ReactNativeDelegate()`, so it runs before React Native and before any JavaScript can open the database. In SDK 58 React Native starts in `SceneDelegate`, after this function, so the order is guaranteed. `swiftc -parse` accepts the generated file.
- **Same folder as expo-sqlite.** `node_modules/expo-sqlite/ios/SQLiteModule.swift` defines `defaultDatabaseDirectory` as `appContext.config.documentDirectory + "SQLite"` (the `cacheDirectory` branch is `tvOS` only, and `app.json` has no tvOS target). `AppContextConfig` sets `documentDirectory` to `FileManager.default.urls(for: .documentDirectory, in: .userDomainMask).first` when no scoped directory is given, which is what the block uses. Same path.
- **Cannot delete data.** The block only calls `createDirectory(withIntermediateDirectories: true)` (a no-op on an existing folder, it does not touch the content) and `setResourceValues`. I ran the block twice on a folder holding a file: the file was still there and the attribute was set (`xattr` showed `com.apple.metadata:com_apple_backup_excludeItem`). Not run on the iOS simulator: that device check of T026 is still the Implementer's or Fernando's.
- **Scope.** The attribute is on the directory, so everything inside it (database, `-wal`, `-shm`) is skipped by iCloud and computer backups, including files created later. It does not cover anything stored outside `Documents/SQLite` (see SEC-T026-3).

### What happens when `setResourceValues` fails

The `catch {}` swallows both `createDirectory` and `setResourceValues` errors. Judgement: acceptable for the behaviour (the plan and constitution say the app never blocks the coach, so it must open), but not acceptable as silent. If the write fails, the folder stays included in iCloud and computer backups and nobody can tell: children's names could reach iCloud without any trace. How likely: low (the call fails on a full disk, a damaged volume or a missing folder, and a missing folder is already handled by `createDirectory`). How long: the block runs at every launch, so it heals itself on the next successful launch, and iCloud backups usually run while the device is idle and charging, so the exposure window is short but real. Safer handling inside the plan (no change to the plan, the failure is still ignored for the user): keep the `do/catch`, but in the `catch` write one fixed message with `NSLog`, for example `NSLog("with-backup-exclusion: the database folder could not be excluded from backups")`. It must not print the error, the path or any name (a path under `Documents` has no player data, but the rule is no data in logs, and a fixed text is simplest to audit). Optionally read the value back after setting it (`resourceValues(forKeys: [.isExcludedFromBackupKey])`) and use the same message if it is not `true`. A test can check that the `catch` is not empty and holds no interpolation.

### Findings

| #          | Severity  | Finding                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Status                                                              |
| ---------- | --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SEC-T026-1 | Important | `catch {}` hides a failed exclusion, so children's data could stay in iCloud and computer backups with no signal. Replace it with a `catch` that writes a fixed `NSLog` message, with no error, path or data, as described above, and add a test for it. The behaviour stays the same for the user (the app still opens).      | Open, for the Implementer before the T026 commit (small, same task) |
| SEC-T026-2 | Minor     | The device check of T026 (`xattr` on the simulator after `prebuild --platform ios`) has not been run. The macOS program proves the API behaviour, not the simulator path. Run it before closing D2, or in the T029 dev build.                                                                                                  | Open, for Fernando or the Implementer                               |
| SEC-T026-3 | Minor     | Only `Documents/SQLite` is excluded. Anything else the app stores later (another folder, a file, `AsyncStorage`, a `directory` argument of `openDatabaseSync`) would be backed up. T029 must open the database with no `directory` argument (the task already says so); the Codex review and the audit checklist now check it. | Open, checked in T029 and later                                     |
| SEC-T026-4 | Minor     | The block is a plain string with no tvOS branch, while expo-sqlite uses the cache folder on tvOS. The app has no tvOS target, so there is no gap today; if one is ever added, revisit.                                                                                                                                         | Accepted                                                            |
| SEC-T026-5 | Minor     | Android `dataExtractionRules` also takes effect only on Android 12+, and `fullBackupContent` on 11 and lower; both files are written, so there is no gap. Android 12+ transfer to a new device is covered by `<device-transfer>`. The E-028 device test (new phone has no data) is still manual.                               | Accepted, manual check in step 9                                    |

### Verdict

- **Blocking:** none.
- **Important:** one (SEC-T026-1), a small hardening of the `catch` inside the same task.
- **Minor:** four (SEC-T026-2 to SEC-T026-5).
- Can T026 be committed? Yes, once the Implementer replaces the empty `catch {}` with the fixed-message handling and its test, and `scripts/verify.sh` is green. The Android part meets principle 5 as it is. The Security files changed by this audit are `docs/security.md` and this section.

## D2 verification (2026-11-13)

- **Scope:** verifier run for D2 close (T061) on branch `001-planificador-v1-d2`, HEAD `8ff01b8`, clean working tree before the run. Nothing was changed, committed or fixed; T057 and T061 remain unticked.
- **Mutation time (D2 rule 4):** the full local run `STRYKER_INCREMENTAL=1 STRYKER_FORCE=1 npm run verify` passed (exit 0) in **6 min 13.8 s** total; Stryker itself took 5 min 20 s (804 mutants, 24 files, 1,161 tests). The incremental run `STRYKER_INCREMENTAL=1 npm run verify`, right after it, passed (exit 0) in **1 min 8.8 s** total; Stryker took 17 s and reused the file. On CI, the first full run after T024, T080, T028 and T029 is still to be recorded (see T061 Done-when).
- **Steps of the full run (all green):** types, lint (`--max-warnings 0`), format, focused or skipped tests (none), Jest with coverage (197 suites, 2,694 tests, all passed), traceability, `drizzle-kit check` ("Everything's fine"), Expo Doctor (20/20), Stryker, `npm audit`, `osv-scanner` (no issues), gitleaks (no leaks in 115 commits), semgrep ("No issues found").
- **Coverage:** global Jest coverage statements 98.78 %, branches 96.32 %, lines 98.93 %. `src/domain/` (24 non-test files, from `coverage/lcov.info`): lines 316/316 = **100.00 %**, branches 172/178 = **96.63 %**. Threshold 90 % met.
- **Mutation score:** `src/domain/` **94.78 %** (467 killed, 23 timed out, 27 survived, 0 no coverage), above the 80 % threshold. Per file, the D2 domain files are: `squad-validation.ts` 100 % (0 survivors), `match-input.ts` 100 % (0), `match-date.ts` 100 % (0). `team-order.ts` has no mutants generated ("n/a", 2 errors): see minor finding M1.
- **Traceability (`node scripts/check-traceability.mjs`):** "Traceability OK (2 specs checked)". My own check, with the same name rules, confirms it for D2: all 86 RF/E IDs on the 43 ticked D2 task lines have a Jest test name, and none is covered only by a Maestro flow. The Maestro-written IDs (E-001, E-003, E-005 … E-032 and the RF-010 … RF-087 listed in `e2e/`) all also have Jest tests. Of the 120 IDs in `spec.md`, 109 have a Jest test name; the 11 without one (RF-015, RF-018, RF-020, RF-021, RF-022, RF-024, RF-028, RF-029, RF-039, RF-074, RF-075) all belong to D3 (T067–T078). **No ID of US1–US4 lacks a test.**
- **Undo survivors (`src/domain/undo.ts`, 8 from the Stryker HTML report; the JSON report is not written, so the list comes from the clear-text output):**
  - L32:45 `/^(P|OT)/` → `/(P|OT)/`: **equivalent**; a column key always starts with `P` or `OT`.
  - L97:8 `...entry.filter(non-X)` → `...entry`, and L97:33 → `filter(() => true)`: **equivalent**; the starter list is then applied again at the end of the entry, so every X still gets its second chance after the non-X changes. Only the order of `changes` can differ.
  - L98:8 and L98:33 (the starter list widened to all changes): **equivalent**; the duplicated non-X changes are no-ops by the line-112 guard.
  - L105:7 `cellState(...) !== "starter"` → `true`: **equivalent**; when the cell is already a starter, the line-112 guard skips it anyway.
  - L112:9 `if (cellState(...) === change.state) continue;` → `if (false) continue;`: **defence only**; it fires only with a history that does not match the grid (a cell already in the state it would take). Real actions cannot build that history (`pruneHistory`), as `plan.md` "Test strategy" documents.
  - L123:20 loop start `history.length - 1` → `history.length + 1`: **equivalent**; the extra index reads `undefined`, is treated as an empty entry and skipped.
  - No undo survivor is left unclassified.
- **Other domain survivors (19, in files not in the documented `undo.ts` list):** `bounded-flow.ts` 9, `marking.ts` 5, `rule-feasibility.ts` 2, `column.ts` 1, `overtime.ts` 1, `player-order.ts` 1. Their total (27) equals the count of the D1 CI run (review.md, D1 verification: 27 survived), so they are probably the same set, but no section records them one by one. See M2.
- **Repo checks:** `git ls-files ios android` is empty; `/ios` and `/android` are in `.gitignore`. No `console.` in `src/`. No `fetch(`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `WebSocket` or `EventSource` in `src/`. No `eslint-disable`, `@ts-ignore` or `@ts-expect-error` in the code (they appear only in docs, the review and coverage output). No `.only` or `.skip` in the tests, except the fixture strings of `tests/scripts/check-focused-tests-patterns.test.ts`, which the check script tests on purpose. Every `src/` file is within 250 counted lines: ESLint `max-lines` (skipping blank and comment lines) passed in the full run; the largest counted file is about 248 lines.
- **Deviations (`plan.md` "Deviations"):** 22 rows in D2. Their status: 20 are approved by Fernando (D1 PR, or chat of 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-13); **2 are still without approval**, recorded as "Implementer's choice, for Fernando's review": (a) 2026-11-13, "FK columns are NOT NULL"; (b) 2026-11-13, "T082 uses the SDK 58 drop-in `DateTimePicker` of @expo/ui in one file instead of two platform files". No row says "pending approval" literally.
- **Maestro (not run):** the verifier did not run the Maestro flows; they need a Release build on the simulator or emulator. The T036, T048 and T056 flows, and the other flows in `e2e/`, are still not run. T061 requires them run before the close.

### Findings

- **Blocking (for the T061 close, not for verify):**
  - B1: the Maestro flows listed in T061 (`us1-teams-and-players`, `us1-form-interruption`, `us2-match-and-call-up`, `us3-planning`, `us3-interruption`, `us4-warnings`) have not been run. Output must be recorded before T061 is ticked.

- **Important:**

  - I2: the 19 non-`undo.ts` survivors are not listed per file in any review section, so the rule "a survivor not previously known is a finding" cannot be checked row by row. The Reviewer should list them with their classification.
- **Minor:**
