**Result:** verify green (full and incremental). No blocking finding from the machine checks; B1 and B2 are gates still open for T061.

## Security · D2 close (2026-11-13)

Full audit of delivery D2 (T024–T061, T080–T086) on branch `001-planificador-v1-d2`, HEAD `a53789a`, clean tree, 85 commits and 385 files against `main`. Role: `.agents/roles/security.md`, checklist: `docs/security.md` "Audit of each change". Method: reading the code and the generated native files, plus the scanners of `scripts/verify.sh` run here. Nothing in the code was changed.

### What was run

| Check                                                                                                    | Result                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high`                                                                | exit 0: 0 high, 0 critical. It lists 1 low and 20 moderate, all reachable only through optional peers (`expo-router` > `@testing-library/react-native` > `jest`, and `expo` CLI tools: `glob`, `minimatch`, `deepmerge`); full `npm audit`: 39 (1 low, 38 moderate), 0 high |
| `osv-scanner scan source --lockfile=package-lock.json`                                                   | "No issues found", exit 0 (10 vulnerabilities filtered by `osv-scanner.toml`)                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `gitleaks git` and `gitleaks dir`                                                                        | 116 commits and the working tree: no leaks                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `semgrep scan` (p/typescript, p/react, p/secrets) on `src`, `scripts` and `plugins`                      | exit 0, no findings                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `npx expo prebuild --no-install --clean` in a temporary copy outside the repository (deleted afterwards) | Android and iOS generated; `git status` of the repository stays clean; no `ios/` or `android/` in the repository (`/ios` and `/android` are in `.gitignore`)                                                                                                                |
| `swiftc -parse` of the generated `AppDelegate.swift`                                                     | accepted, no errors                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

### 1. Dependencies

- `package.json` has no dependency added after T024 (audited in "Security · T024"): the diff against `main` holds exactly the T024 set (`expo-sqlite`, `drizzle-orm`, `zustand`; dev: `drizzle-kit`, `better-sqlite3`, `@types/better-sqlite3`) and two Expo patch bumps. `package-lock.json` changed in T024 and T027 only.
- `better-sqlite3` is `devOptional` in the lockfile (optional peer of `drizzle-orm`). `npm ls --omit=dev` still shows it under `drizzle-orm` because npm lists optional peers. It cannot reach the app: nothing in `src/` imports it, and the package has no `android/`, `ios/`, `expo-module.config.json` or `react-native.config.js`, so Expo autolinking does not link it.
- `osv-scanner.toml`: 8 `[[IgnoredVulns]]` entries (10 advisories with aliases), every one with reason, expiry and Fernando's approval (2026-11-10 and 2026-11-13). Expiry: 7 entries on 2027-02-08 (90 days from 2026-11-10) and the esbuild one (GHSA-67mh-4wv8-2f99) on 2027-02-10 (89 days). All within the 90-day limit and not expired today. All are development or build tools; none is in the app bundle. SEC-T024-4 stays: renew or remove all together before 2027-02-08, otherwise CI turns red on that day.
- `.github/workflows/verify.yml`: `permissions: contents: read`, actions pinned to full SHAs, gitleaks and osv-scanner downloaded with SHA-256 checks, semgrep pinned. `.github/dependabot.yml` covers `github-actions` only (npm updates are by hand on purpose). No finding.

### 2. Backup plugin (`plugins/with-backup-exclusion.js`)

Generated output confirmed, same as in "Security · T026":

- **Android manifest:** `<application>` has `android:allowBackup="false"`, `android:dataExtractionRules="@xml/data_extraction_rules"` and `android:fullBackupContent="@xml/full_backup_content"`, once each. `app.json` has `android.allowBackup: false` and lists the plugin once. Both XML files exclude the nine domains; `data_extraction_rules.xml` has `<cloud-backup>` and `<device-transfer>`.
- **iOS `AppDelegate.swift`:** the tagged block `with-backup-exclusion-ios` appears once (begin and end markers), inside `application(_:didFinishLaunchingWithOptions:)` and before `let delegate = ReactNativeDelegate()`. It builds the folder from `.documentDirectory` + `"SQLite"`, creates it, sets `isExcludedFromBackup`, deletes nothing. SEC-T026-1 is fixed: the `catch` writes the fixed `NSLog("Backup exclusion of the database folder failed")` with no error, path or data (`tests/plugins/with-backup-exclusion-ios-logging.test.ts` covers it).
- **Same folder as the database:** `src/data/database.ts:19` calls `openDatabaseSync(DATABASE_NAME)` with one argument; `src/data/database.test.ts:67` asserts that no `directory` argument is passed (SEC-T026-3 closed for D2). No other place stores data on the device: `git grep` finds no `AsyncStorage`, `SecureStore`, `expo-file-system`, `localStorage` or `MMKV` in `src/`.

### 3. Data layer (`src/data/*`)

- **Queries:** only Drizzle builders (`select/insert/update/delete` with `eq`, `and`, `inArray`, `onConflictDoUpdate`); all values are bound parameters. The only `sql` templates: `src/data/grid-repository.ts:139` (`sql\`${matches.overtimeCount} + 1\``, a column reference, no input) and the CHECK constraints in `src/data/schema.ts:25,58,61,105,107`(static text). No SQL string built by concatenation or interpolation (regex over`src/`found none). The only raw`execSync`calls are the two fixed PRAGMAs at`src/data/database.ts:21-22`.
- **Pragmas:** `foreign_keys = ON` and `journal_mode = WAL` are set before `migrate` (`database.test.ts:82,96,104` prove it). The WAL and shared-memory files live in the same `SQLite` folder, so the iOS exclusion covers them. `migrations/bundle.ts` is generated by `scripts/bundle-migrations.mjs` from the journal and the SQL files; the script uses Node built-ins only, reads everything before writing and renames a temporary file, so it never leaves a half-written bundle. The migration SQL has only `CREATE TABLE`/`CREATE INDEX`.
- **Rollback:** multi-row writes run in `db.transaction` (`match-repository.ts:36`, `grid-repository.ts:101,149`, `call-up-repository.ts:45`); a throw rolls back. `openAppDatabase` closes the client and rethrows if a pragma or the migration fails (`database.ts:27-30`). Ownership of entries is checked before the first write (`grid-repository.ts:62-83`).
- **No logging:** no `console`, `NSLog` or log call in `src/`; `eslint.config.js:125` has `no-console: error`. Errors are swallowed into `unchanged`/`saveFailed`/`notFound` states with fixed texts, so no error message with SQL or names reaches the screen.
- **Layers:** `src/data` may import only `expo-sqlite` and `drizzle-orm` (`eslint.config.js:44`); `app` and `ui` may not (`:52`). `src/data/database.source.test.ts` checks that `expo-sqlite` is imported only from `src/data/`.

### 4. Navigation and screens

- **Route parameters:** every dynamic route reads its id with `useLocalSearchParams` and parses it through `parseTeamId` / `parseMatchId` (`src/state/route-params.ts:9-17`): only the canonical text of a positive safe integer is accepted; arrays, `01`, `1e3`, `+1`, `-1` and spaces give `null` and the screen shows `NotFound`. Files: `teams/[teamId]/index.tsx:17`, `edit.tsx:15`, `players/new.tsx:15`, `matches/new.tsx:15`, `matches/[matchId]/index.tsx:82`, `call-up.tsx:23`, `new-player.tsx:16`.
- **Routes carry only numeric ids.** Every `router.push`/`replace` builds `/teams/${id}`, `/matches/${id}/...` from a parsed or database id; names, categories and notes are never in a path or query string. A team name appears only as the native header title (`teams/[teamId]/index.tsx`, `Stack.Screen options`), which is not part of any URL.
- **Deep links (`planificadorperiodos://`):** the scheme opens the routes `/`, `/teams/new`, `/teams/<id>`, `/teams/<id>/edit`, `/teams/<id>/players/new`, `/teams/<id>/matches/new`, `/matches/<id>`, `/matches/<id>/call-up`, `/matches/<id>/new-player`. On open they only read (`openTeam`, `openMatch`, `loadTeams`: `select` only; the new-match draft `reset` is in memory). Every write (`createTeam`, `updateTeam`, `addPlayer`, `createMatch`, `addToCallUp`, `removeFromCallUp`, `createPlayerInMatch`, `saveCellChanges`, `addOvertime`, `removeLastOvertime`) is called only from a button `onPress`, a form submit or a cell tap. No route deletes anything on open. The call-up and new-player routes also refuse a link whose id differs from the open session (`view.matchId !== id`, `call-up.tsx:30`, `new-player.tsx:21`), so a crafted link cannot act on another match. Until the database is open, `_layout.tsx:26` renders nothing, so no link reaches a screen too early. An unmatched path shows Expo Router's default unmatched screen with the typed path only (no data).
- **Network and SDKs:** no `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `WebSocket`, `EventSource`, `Linking`, `WebBrowser` or analytics in `src/`; `eslint.config.js:126-140` forbids the four globals (also through `globalThis`/`window`/`self`/`global`). `expo-web-browser`, `expo-device`, `expo-constants`, `expo-linking` are installed (template) but imported by no file in `src/`. No third-party SDK, no telemetry.
- **Zustand (SEC-T024-3 closed):** stores use `zustand/vanilla` `createStore` and `useStore` only (`src/state/*-store.ts`, `new-match-draft.ts`); no `devtools`, `persist` or `zustand/middleware` anywhere.
- **expo-sqlite dev tools:** the only `expo-sqlite` import is `src/data/database.ts`; no `SQLiteProvider`, `useSQLiteContext`, change listener or `__DEV__` code in `src/`. The package's own dev client returns at once outside `__DEV__` (T024 audit).
- **Names in the UI only:** the player name is interpolated into a confirmation title (`call-up.tsx:62`, `t("confirm.removeFromCallUp.title", { name })`). `escapeValue: false` in `src/i18n/index.ts:12` is correct for React Native Text (no HTML) and the text is not logged or sent anywhere.

### 5. Configuration

- `app.json`: scheme `planificadorperiodos`; no `permissions` list, no `extra`, no `updates`, no keys; plugins: `expo-router`, `expo-splash-screen`, `expo-status-bar`, `expo-web-browser`, `expo-sqlite` (no options), `./plugins/with-backup-exclusion.js`. `ios.supportsTablet: true`; no `eas.json`.
- **Generated Android manifest** (debug-oriented prebuild): permissions `INTERNET`, `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW`, `VIBRATE`, `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` and `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (both `maxSdkVersion=32`). They are Expo's baseline permissions plus the dev client, not requested by app code. A second intent-filter scheme `exp+planificador-periodos` comes from `expo-dev-client`. See finding SEC-D2-1.
- **Generated iOS `Info.plist`:** `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription`, `NSBonjourServices` (`_expo._tcp`), `NSAllowsLocalNetworking` and the `exp+planificador-periodos` URL scheme come from `expo-dev-client`; `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads` is `false`. `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption` is not set. See SEC-D2-1 and SEC-D2-2.
- `.gitignore` covers `/ios`, `/android`, `.env*.local`, `*.jks`, `*.p8`, `*.p12`, `*.key`, `*.mobileprovision`, `*.pem`, `coverage/`, `reports/`. `git ls-files` shows no `.env`, key, certificate, `.db`, `.sqlite` or Firebase file.

### 6. Tests and support

- `better-sqlite3` is imported by three files, all under `tests/` and never by `src/` or a route: `tests/support/test-database.ts:1` (the in-memory database helper, as planned), `tests/data/expo-sqlite-double.ts:9` and `tests/data/migration-sql.test.ts:5`. SEC-T024-2 asked that no file of `src/` or the app imports it, and that `drizzle-orm` is imported only through its `better-sqlite3` subpaths in the helper: both hold (`tests/data/test-database.test.ts:81-102` checks them on the source text). Justification of the second one: Jest has no native `expo-sqlite`, and `src/data/database.ts` must be tested with the real `openDatabaseSync`, pragmas and migrator; the double is registered with `jest.mock("expo-sqlite")` and runs on a real SQLite file so WAL and foreign keys behave as on a device. The third one (`migration-sql.test.ts`) is a test that runs the SQL files directly. None of the three is reachable from the bundle, because Metro follows imports from `src/app/` and no `src/` file imports `tests/`. `tests/data/expo-sqlite-double.ts` writes only to a `mkdtemp` folder in the OS temporary directory.
- Test data: only invented first names (`Ana`, `Bea`, `Eva`, `Marta`, `Juan Carlos`) and numbers; no real names, no emails, no keys. gitleaks is clean over the whole history.
- Maestro flows (`e2e/*.yaml`): use `clearState`, the app id and one `openLink: planificadorperiodos://matches/1`; no credentials or URLs.

### Findings

| #        | Severity                      | Finding                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Fix                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| -------- | ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SEC-D2-1 | Important, before the release | `app.json` has no `android.blockedPermissions`. The prebuilt manifest asks for `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW`, `VIBRATE`, `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` and `INTERNET`, none used by the app (offline, no files, no overlay). The iOS plist also carries the dev-client local-network keys and the `exp+planificador-periodos` scheme. Not exploitable in D2 (no release build exists, no `eas.json`), but principle 3 and "Unnecessary permissions" need them gone in the published build. | Before the first release, add `android.blockedPermissions` (the four above, and `INTERNET` for the production profile only, because Metro needs it in development), build a Release/production profile without `expo-dev-client` effects, review the merged manifest and plist, and run MobSF. Already on the "Before each release" list; two items added there. |
| SEC-D2-2 | Minor, before the release     | `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption` is not in `Info.plist`; App Store Connect will ask on every upload. The app uses no encryption of its own.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Set `ios.config.usesNonExemptEncryption: false` in `app.json` when the first iOS build is prepared.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| SEC-D2-3 | Minor (not a leak)            | `src/app/_layout.tsx:16-21`: if `initializeDatabase()` rejects (failed migration, full disk), the error is swallowed (`() => undefined`), `ready` stays false, the layout renders `null` and `hideAsync()` is never called, so the coach sees the splash screen forever with no message. No data is exposed, and swallowing the error is right for privacy (no error text), but the app is unusable and silent. It is not covered by a spec text, so it is a product decision, not a Security change.  | Ask Fernando; if wanted, show a fixed Spanish message from `es.json` (no error text, no path) and call `hideAsync()` in the failure branch.                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| SEC-D2-4 | Minor                         | The 8 `osv-scanner.toml` entries expire on 2027-02-08 and 2027-02-10 (SEC-T024-1 and SEC-T024-4 still open): the `ignoreUntil` key is deprecated in favour of `effectiveUntil`; on those dates CI fails if nothing is renewed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Renew or drop them together before 2027-02-08, and migrate to `effectiveUntil` then.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| SEC-D2-5 | Minor, not verified           | SEC-T026-2 (the `xattr` check on the iOS simulator or a device) is still open: Fernando's, as agreed. Nothing here proves the attribute is set on a real install. The Android E-028 test (a new phone receives no data) is also manual.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Fernando: after the first iOS dev build, `xattr -l <app container>/Documents/SQLite` must show `com.apple.metadata:com_apple_backup_excludeItem`; at T061 record the result.                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| SEC-D2-6 | Minor, informational          | `package-lock.json` marks `better-sqlite3` as `devOptional` and `npm ls --omit=dev` lists it through the optional peer of `drizzle-orm`. It is harmless today (no native code to autolink, nothing imports it), but a future change that imports it from `src/` would be caught only by `tests/data/test-database.test.ts:81`.                                                                                                                                                                         | None now. The existing test is the control.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

### What I could NOT verify

- **No device or simulator.** I did not run the app, the Maestro flows, an iOS simulator or an Android emulator. So not verified: that the `isExcludedFromBackup` attribute is really set on the simulator or device (SEC-T026-2 / SEC-D2-5); that Android honours the rule files on a real backup and transfer; that the `NSLog` line appears when the write fails; that a deep link on a device opens the routes described (read from the code and the tests only).
- **Release artefacts.** There is no release build, so I did not run MobSF on an APK or an IPA, nor inspect a release manifest or `Info.plist`; the manifest and plist above are from `prebuild`, which includes the dev client. The "Before each release" list stays open.
- **GDPR and store declarations** ("collects no data") are release tasks, not checked here.
- **Codex review** (`codex-review.md`) was not read; this audit is independent of it.

### Verdict

- **Blocking:** none.
- **Important:** one (SEC-D2-1), for the first release, not for D2.
- **Minor:** five (SEC-D2-2 to SEC-D2-6).
- From a security standpoint, D2 can be published (merged and used in development builds). Principles 3 and 5 hold in the code: no network, no player data in logs or URLs, parameterised queries, backups excluded on both platforms (generated output checked), no write on opening a route. The remaining gates are device checks (SEC-D2-5) and the release list, not D2 code. Files changed by this audit: this section and `docs/security.md` (checklist items added).

## Codex review · D2 (2026-11-13)

Run: `scripts/review.sh specs/001-planificador-v1` at commit `883d584` (branch `001-planificador-v1-d2` against `main`), 2026-11-13 18:46. Full output: [codex-review.md](codex-review.md).

Summary: 6 findings in total. BLOCKING 4, IMPORTANT 1, MINOR 1. Codex verdict: changes requested. Status of all six: Open.

- **F1 · BLOCKING · An unknown team can reuse and save another team's abandoned draft.** File: `src/state/new-match-draft.ts:101`. Codex: `reset()` returns without clearing the previous team identity or fields when the requested team does not exist. The route checks only whether the ID is syntactically valid. Reproduced: reset for team A, enter unsaved text, reset for nonexistent team 9999, then `create()` saves the abandoned draft under team A. Breaks RF-082 and the planned not-found handling. Proposed fix: invalidate the draft before loading, handle missing teams and read failures explicitly, and render `NotFound` unless initialization succeeds. Add a regression test covering an abandoned draft followed by an unknown-team route.
- **F2 · BLOCKING · Grid sizing can disable scrolling while rows are clipped.** File: `src/ui/grid/match-grid.tsx:35` and `src/app/matches/[matchId]/index.tsx:114`. Codex: layout uses the entire window height, but the screen never supplies `chromeHeight`; it also omits the call-up button and phone toolbar. For a 1024 × 768 tablet with 12 players, a 44-point header and a 24-point legend, it produces 716 points of grid content with vertical scrolling disabled. Lower rows become unreachable. The isolated layout tests supply heights the real screen does not supply. RF-076 to RF-081 are therefore not satisfied. Proposed fix: measure the actual grid viewport and derive sizes and scrolling from it. Adjust the surrounding controls and warning layout to preserve the tablet's 12-row guarantee. Test the assembled screen with realistic measured heights.
- **F3 · BLOCKING · A failure after a committed write leaves saved data and published state inconsistent.** File: `src/state/squad-store.ts:118` and `src/state/match-session-marks.ts:40`. Codex: actions perform fallible reads after their repository transaction has committed. Reproduced: `createTeam()` returns `saveFailed` after `listTeams()` throws, but one team is already stored; retrying creates a duplicate. A tap followed by a failing `listPlayers()` saves the mark, throws, leaves the displayed grid empty, records no Undo entry and leaves `saveFailed` false. Call-up publication has the same pattern. Contradicts RF-085 and the documented save/publish guarantees. Proposed fix: prepare publication before committing, or include all fallible preparation in the transaction and publish only after success. Do not merely catch the post-commit exception. Add tests that fail the reads following successful writes, including retry and Undo assertions.
- **F4 · BLOCKING · Players created from a match do not appear when returning to the team screen.** File: `src/app/teams/[teamId]/index.tsx:21`. Codex: the team loads only on mount. Creating a player from the match updates SQLite and the match session, while the mounted team screen retains its old squad store. Reproduced: SQLite contains two players sharing number 7, but the squad store still publishes one player and no duplicate warning. Returning through Back preserves that stale screen. Breaks RF-035 and RF-069. Proposed fix: refresh the team on focus or update its store after successful player creation. Add a complete team, match, new player, Back-to-team test checking both the player and the duplicate warning.
- **F5 · IMPORTANT · Device-dependent tasks are marked complete without their required results.** File: `specs/001-planificador-v1/tasks.md:881`. Codex: T036, T048, T056 and T060 are ticked although their completion criteria require successful platform runs, and their reports explicitly say those runs never happened. Static YAML checks do not establish native picker, interruption, scrolling or performance behaviour. Proposed fix: keep those tasks pending until the required outputs are recorded, or explicitly revise their completion criteria through the approved workflow.
- **F6 · MINOR · The summary reports a nonexistent missing text.** File: `specs/001-planificador-v1/summary.md:311`. Codex: it says the spec lacks `grid.addOvertime`, but that row exists at `spec.md:500`. Proposed fix: remove the stale claim from the summary and the corresponding task report.

Codex's category results: RF/E implementation, tests and scope (F1 to F5; full spec incomplete through approved D3 deferrals; no unrelated product additions). Constitution, layers, conventions and glossary (F1 to F4 affect specified behaviour and persistence guarantees; no additional findings). Test integrity (F1 to F5 expose coverage gaps; no skipped tests; the deleted placeholder home-screen test has recorded authorisation in T033). Security (no additional findings; device backup and release-permission checks remain unverified). Explanation accuracy (F6; persistence guarantees overstated given F3). Simplicity and efficiency (no findings).

Codex's checks: TypeScript, ESLint, traceability, focused-test checks and 1,166 domain cases through an in-memory runner passed. The state reproductions used real in-memory SQLite. Standard Jest was blocked by cache writes in the read-only environment; coverage, Stryker, full verification and device behaviour were not independently confirmed by Codex.

### Verdict (Codex review · D2)

Not approved for closing. Four BLOCKING findings (F1 to F4) are open and must be resolved, through phase 6, before phase 8 can pass again. F5 (IMPORTANT) needs a fix or the developer's recorded acceptance as pending. F6 (MINOR) is a suggestion.

F1: fixed in 802e63c
F4: fixed in f2001a7

F3: fixed in ae302f3

F2: fixed in 9502f00

## Codex review · D2 re-run (2026-11-13)

Run: `scripts/review.sh specs/001-planificador-v1` at commit `840d72e` (branch `001-planificador-v1-d2` against `main`), 2026-11-13 19:31. Full output: [codex-review.md](codex-review.md). The first run's output is kept in the scratchpad only; its findings are the ones listed above (F1 to F6).

Summary: 4 findings in total. BLOCKING 2, IMPORTANT 1, MINOR 1. Codex verdict: **changes requested (not approved)**.

- **BLOCKING · Android assigns a time without user selection.** File: `src/ui/date-time-field.tsx:59`. Codex: the inline time picker is always mounted, using 12:00 when `time` is null. The installed `@expo/ui` Android implementation emits its initial value from `LaunchedEffect`; this handler immediately stores it. Consequently, an untouched match can save 12:00, and clearing a previously selected time can restore 12:00. This violates RF-025 and RF-083/E-032. The test double emits only simulated user selections, hiding the defect. Proposed fix: open the Android picker explicitly, commit only a confirmed selection, and unmount it after confirmation or dismissal. Test initialization and clearing with native-equivalent callbacks.
- **BLOCKING · The tablet's 12-row guarantee remains broken.** File: `src/app/matches/[matchId]/index.tsx:111`. Codex: measuring the viewport fixes unreachable rows, but the surrounding controls still consume too much height. On the reference 1024 × 768 tablet, the call-up button, warning banner, vertical legend and headers leave fewer than the 528 points required for twelve 44-point rows. A six-period match with twelve players and one player marked in P1–P4 demonstrates this state. RF-081 requires the twelve rows to fit without scrolling. The new tests explicitly accept scrolling at these smaller viewports instead of enforcing that requirement. Proposed fix: compact the tablet controls, banners and legend; test the assembled screen with warnings present and assert twelve visible rows without scrolling. Previous finding F2 is only partially resolved.
- **IMPORTANT · Team and player forms cannot scroll around the keyboard or overflow.** Files: `src/ui/team-form.tsx:51`, `src/ui/player-form.tsx:41`. Codex: both forms use a fixed `View` without scrolling or keyboard avoidance. On short landscape viewports, particularly with validation messages or the keyboard visible, lower controls extend beyond the usable area without a way to scroll them into view. This undermines RF-076's orientation support. Proposed fix: use keyboard-aware, scrollable form containers. Verify that fields, errors and Save remain reachable in landscape with the keyboard open.
- **MINOR · Mutation cache excludes test setup files.** File: `.github/workflows/verify.yml:60`. Codex: the cache hashes helpers and configurations but omits `tests/setup/**`, including `fast-check-mutation.ts`, which Stryker loads. Changes to property-test configuration can therefore reuse stale mutant results. Proposed fix: add `tests/setup/**` consistently to the restore key, restore prefix and save key.

Codex's category results: RF/E coverage and scope (findings 1 to 3; full v1 remains incomplete through the approved D3 deferrals; no unrelated product additions). Constitution, layers, conventions and glossary (no additional findings). Test integrity (findings 1 and 2 expose coverage gaps; finding 4 affects verification; no unapproved deletion, skipping or weakening; the placeholder test deletion has recorded authorisation). Security (no new application-security findings; native backup and production-permission checks remain pending). Explanation accuracy (the assertion that F2 is fixed overstates the result, see finding 2). Simplicity and efficiency (no findings).

Codex's validation: TypeScript, ESLint, source formatting, traceability and focused/skipped-test checks passed. All 1,166 domain cases passed through an in-memory runner. Gitleaks passed. Standard Jest was blocked by cache writes in the read-only environment; coverage, Stryker, full verification and device behaviour were not independently confirmed.

### Verdict (Codex review · D2 re-run)

Not approved for closing. Two BLOCKING findings are open (Android time default; tablet 12-row guarantee, which also means F2 is only partially resolved). One IMPORTANT (form scrolling) and one MINOR (mutation cache key) are open. Status of all four: Open.

forms: fixed in ab6fe40, cache key: fixed in 190e524 (IMPORTANT and MINOR of the re-run; pending Codex confirmation).

Android time: fixed in 4d809eb

tablet 12 rows: fixed in e70d2d8

## Codex review · D2 third run (2026-11-14)

Run: `scripts/review.sh specs/001-planificador-v1` at commit `3e03a1e` (branch `001-planificador-v1-d2` against `main`), 2026-11-14 08:46. Full output: [codex-review.md](codex-review.md). The previous run's output is kept in the scratchpad only.

Summary: 5 findings in total. BLOCKING 4, IMPORTANT 0, MINOR 1. Codex verdict: **changes requested (not approved)**. Status of all five: Open.

- **1 · BLOCKING · Returning from another match link leaves the original grid unavailable.** File: `src/app/matches/[matchId]/index.tsx:80`. Codex: Reproduced: open match 1, navigate to `/matches/999`, then Back. The pathname returns to match 1, but the shared session is `closed`; its mounted screen never reloads. Proposed fix: guard cleanup by the owning match ID and restore the requested session when focus returns with a missing or different session. Preserve Undo when returning from child routes. Add this navigation regression test.
- **2 · BLOCKING · The save-error banner overlaps «Guardar».** Files: `src/ui/save-error-banner.tsx:17`, `src/ui/team-form.tsx:77`, `src/ui/player-form.tsx:65`. Codex: The banner now sits inside the scroll content but remains absolutely positioned at its bottom. Since Save is the last element in normal flow, the banner covers it after a failure. Existing tests invoke its handler directly and cannot detect the overlap. Proposed fix: render form errors in normal flow, or reserve space for the overlay. Verify that Save remains visible and physically tappable after failure.
- **3 · BLOCKING · The 12-row guarantee excludes supported smaller tablets.** File: `src/ui/grid/tablet-budget.ts:9`. Codex: The budget assumes 768 points is the shortest tablet height, while the approved tablet threshold is 600. Direct evaluation at 900 × 600, with twelve players, produces `verticalScroll: true`, violating RF-081. Proposed fix: cover the 600-point boundary in both orientations and reconcile the layout with the twelve-row requirement. If the planned 44-point minimum row height makes that impossible, resolve that constraint through the specification workflow.
- **4 · BLOCKING · Compact warnings can become permanently unreadable.** File: `src/ui/warnings/warning-strip.tsx:57`. Codex: The strip caps content at 80 points and hides overflow, while the legend wraps without a height limit. Additional wrapping, particularly in portrait or with larger system text, clips required explanations without any way to reveal them, contrary to RF-087. Proposed fix: make all warning content reachable within the bounded strip, or use a responsive layout that preserves it. Test rendered bounds and accessibility, beyond checking that Text nodes exist.
- **5 · MINOR · The picker explanation describes the previous implementation.** File: `specs/001-planificador-v1/summary.md:153`. Codex: It says there is one picker file and Android is always open. Android now uses a separate component and explicitly opened dialogs. Proposed fix: update this paragraph to match the current implementation.

Codex's category results: RF/E coverage and scope (findings 1 to 4; D3 omissions are approved deferrals; no unrelated product additions found). Security (no new findings from static inspection; production permissions and device backup verification remain pending as documented). Explanation accuracy (finding 5; reachability claims also need adjustment following findings 2 to 4). Simplicity and efficiency (no additional findings).

## Codex review · D2 fourth run (2026-11-14)

Run: `scripts/review.sh specs/001-planificador-v1` at commit `bf8bf3b` (branch `001-planificador-v1-d2` against `main`), 2026-11-14 09:22. Full output: [codex-review.md](codex-review.md). The previous run's output is kept in the scratchpad only.

Summary: 5 findings in total. BLOCKING 3, IMPORTANT 1, MINOR 1. Codex verdict: **changes requested (not approved)**. Status of all five: Open.

- **1 · BLOCKING · SQLite exposes the database through development tooling.** File: `src/data/database.ts:19`. Codex: `openDatabaseSync(DATABASE_NAME)` automatically registers this connection with Expo's development inspector. The installed implementation opens a WebSocket and supports exporting the database and returning query results, including player names. This violates RF-011 and constitution principles 3 and 5 in the development builds used by this project. Expo documents that the inspector is [automatically enabled in development](https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v58.0.0/sdk/sqlite/#browse-an-on-device-database). Proposed fix: prevent this database from registering with DevTools—for the installed version, `useNewConnection: true` bypasses registration—and test that opening it under `__DEV__` creates no inspector connection or export handlers. Update the security report accordingly.

- **2 · BLOCKING · Returning to a form can save a match for another team.** File: `src/app/teams/[teamId]/matches/new.tsx:22`. Codex: Every mounted form shares one draft, but `ready` is initialized only once. Reproduced through the real routes: open team 1's form, push team 2's form, press Back, then «Guardar». The pathname belongs to team 1, while SQLite stores the match under team 2. The regression assertion fails with **expected team 1, received team 2**. This breaks RF-025 and RF-082. Proposed fix: give each form its own draft, or restore and validate draft ownership when focus returns. Add this two-team navigation regression, asserting both the saved team and call-up.

- **3 · BLOCKING · Native pickers are not constrained to Spanish.** Files: `src/ui/date-time-field.tsx:34`, `src/ui/date-time-field-android.tsx:35`. Codex: i18next controls the surrounding labels, but the native controls inherit their platform locale. The iOS picker receives no `locale`; Android uses the device configuration and default system button labels. On an English-configured device, the form therefore includes English native UI, contrary to RF-012 and constitution principle 7. The picker doubles conceal this. Proposed fix: configure Spanish native localization, supply the supported picker locale and translated Android button labels, and verify on devices configured in English. The [SDK 58 picker API](https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v58.0.0/sdk/ui/drop-in-replacements/datetimepicker/) exposes these locale and button-label controls.

- **4 · IMPORTANT · The floating save error overlaps the phone toolbar.** Files: `src/ui/save-error-banner.tsx:31`, `src/app/matches/[matchId]/index.tsx:147`. Codex: The phone toolbar occupies the screen's bottom edge. The error banner is rendered afterward, absolutely positioned 16 points above that same edge, without reserved space. After a failed operation, it covers part of the toolbar labels and intercepts taps in that area. The form correction did not address this placement. Proposed fix: position the banner above the toolbar or reserve its space. Verify physical button reachability after failed Undo and overtime operations.

- **5 · MINOR · The learning explanation still shows the removed session implementation.** Files: `specs/001-planificador-v1/summary.md:222`, `specs/001-planificador-v1/tasks.md:878`. Codex: The summary describes a `useState` initializer and unconditional cleanup; T084 says no focus event is used. The current implementation uses `useFocusEffect` and ownership-guarded cleanup. Proposed fix: replace the obsolete snippet and update T084 and the corresponding plan explanation to describe `useOwnSession`.

Codex's category results: RF/E implementation, tests and scope (findings 1 to 4; no unrelated product additions found; D3 and device verification remain pending). Constitution, layers, conventions and glossary (findings 1 and 3 violate constitutional requirements; no additional structural findings). Test integrity and effectiveness (coverage gaps above; no skipped tests or unapproved weakening found; placeholder-test deletion has recorded authorization). Security (finding 1; no additional query-parameterization, application-log or dependency-justification findings; release-permission and backup checks remain pending). Explanation accuracy (finding 5; the security assurance also needs correction following finding 1). Simplicity and efficiency (no additional findings).

Codex's validation: TypeScript, ESLint, source and configuration formatting, traceability, focused/skipped-test detection and Gitleaks passed. With cache writes disabled in memory, 2,310 existing tests passed; nine startup tests were blocked by temporary-file permissions. Full verification, coverage, Stryker and device behaviour were not independently confirmed. No repository files were changed.

### Verdict (Codex review · D2 fourth run)

Not approved for closing. Three BLOCKING findings (1 to 3) and one IMPORTANT finding (4) are open and must be resolved, through phase 6, before phase 8 can pass again. Finding 5 (MINOR) is a suggestion. Status of all five: Open.

Findings 1 and 3: fixed in cb7999c and ed00114 (2026-11-14). Not verified on a device: the inspector behaviour and the pickers' language (Android calendar names follow the device language: residual risk in `plan.md` Deviations).

The final action extends beyond the screen; existing tests check button presence without checking bounds. Proposed fix: allow wrapping or distribute available width between responsive buttons. Verify all labels and touch areas at 375 points with an overtime present.

Codex: The summary still says errors float on match/call-up screens, while they now use normal flow. The plan and T084 still prohibit focus events, while `useOwnSession` uses `useFocusEffect`. Proposed fix: update these passages to describe the final banner placement and ownership-guarded session restoration.

## Codex review · D2 sixth run (2026-11-15)

Run: `scripts/review.sh specs/001-planificador-v1` at commit `582fedf` (branch `001-planificador-v1-d2` against `main`), 2026-11-15 09:14. Full output: [codex-review.md](codex-review.md). The previous run's output is kept in the scratchpad only.

Codex: On phones, warnings have neither a height bound nor a surrounding scroll container. In a 667 × 375 window, a six-period match with 13 players and one player marked in P1–P4 displays both banners and three legend entries. Together with the call-up button, toolbar and navigation header, these consume the grid's available height. Enabling scrolling inside the collapsed grid cannot make its cells reachable. This violates RF-010, RF-076 and RF-080. Existing tests inject viewport heights and miss this surrounding layout problem. Proposed fix: bound and scroll the phone warnings while reserving usable grid space. Verify this assembled scenario in landscape, including after a save failure.

Codex: These passages still describe an overlay error banner, a 600-point tablet threshold and approvals as pending. The implementation uses normal flow and 768 points; later documentation records the approvals. Proposed fix: update current design, completion criteria and pending lists. Keep superseded decisions explicitly marked as historical.

## Codex review · D2 seventh run (2026-11-15)

Run: `scripts/review.sh specs/001-planificador-v1` at commit `7bbbf37` (branch `001-planificador-v1-d2` against `main`), 2026-11-15 09:53. Full output: [codex-review.md](codex-review.md). The previous run's output is kept in the scratchpad only.

Summary: 2 findings in total. BLOCKING 1, IMPORTANT 0, MINOR 1. Codex verdict: **changes requested (not approved)**. Status of both: Fixed in `c2ea193` (finding 1: each mounted form owns the draft by `formId`) and in the following docs commit (finding 2).

- **1 · BLOCKING · Returning to another form for the same team can silently reuse an already-created match.** Files: `src/app/teams/[teamId]/matches/new.tsx:31`, `src/state/new-match-draft.ts:165`. Codex: Reproduced through the real routes and in-memory SQLite: open team 1's new-match form, push another new-match form for team 1, save it, return from its grid to the first form, then press Save. Only one match exists: the first form reopens the previously created match. Draft ownership checks only `teamId`, so returning to the first form skips initialization and retains the second form's cached `created` result. Subsequent saves bypass validation and persistence. This breaks RF-025 and RF-082. Existing navigation tests cover different teams and miss this case. Proposed fix: give each mounted form its own draft, or track ownership with a unique form instance identifier. On returning to a displaced form, initialize its draft and clear the unrelated saved result. Add a regression that edits fields after returning and verifies a distinct match with those values.
- **2 · MINOR · Current explanations still contain superseded statements.** Files: `specs/001-planificador-v1/summary.md:343`, `specs/001-planificador-v1/tasks.md:834`. Codex: The summary says a sixth review never happened. T053 still describes 600 points as the tablet boundary and puts landscape-phone buttons in the toolbar. Current code uses 768 points and places landscape-phone buttons in the header. Proposed fix: update the current summary and task scenarios; explicitly label any retained earlier decisions as historical.

Codex's category results: RF/E implementation and scope (finding 1; D3 requirements remain approved deferrals; no unrelated additions found). Constitution, layers, conventions and glossary (finding 1 affects saving guarantees; no additional structural findings). Test integrity and effectiveness (finding 1 exposes missing coverage; no unapproved deletion, skipping or weakening found). Security (no additional findings from static inspection; device backup and production-build checks remain pending). Explanation accuracy (finding 2). Simplicity and efficiency (no findings).

Codex's validation: types, lint, source and configuration formatting, traceability, focused-test detection and Gitleaks passed. With Jest cache writes disabled in memory, 2,596 existing tests passed; 25 startup tests were blocked by temporary-file permissions. The supplemental navigation regression failed as described. Full verification, coverage, mutation testing and device behaviour were not independently confirmed. No repository files were changed.

Not approved for closing when the run ended. Finding 1 (BLOCKING) fixed in `c2ea193` and finding 2 (MINOR) fixed in the docs commit after it; a new phase 8 run is needed.

## Security · T087 and T088 (D2, 2026-11-14)

Scope: commits `4090040` (T087: message and Retry when the database fails to open) and `cdb06d3` (T088: blocked Android permissions). Method: read the diffs and the final files; `npx expo prebuild --platform android --no-install --clean` in two temporary copies outside the repository (no `ios/` or `android/` was created in the repository), one without `APP_VARIANT` and one with `APP_VARIANT=production`; `npm audit`, OSV-Scanner, gitleaks and Semgrep as `scripts/verify.sh` runs them. No code was changed.

### T087 · message and Retry

- **No data on screen or in logs.** `DatabaseOpenFailed` (`src/ui/database-open-failed.tsx`) shows two fixed texts from `es.json` (`db.openFailed`, `db.retry`) and takes no error prop. `useDatabaseStart` (`src/state/use-database-start.ts:34-36`) drops the rejection reason (`() => finish("failed")`) and returns only `status`, `retrying` and `retry`. No `console` anywhere in `src/` (grep, and the source test). Nothing in `src/`, `plugins/` or `app.config.js` uses `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest` or `WebSocket`.
- **Nothing is deleted or reset.** No `deleteDatabase*` in `src/`; `openAppDatabase` still opens the same name `planificador.db`, and on failure only closes its own connection (`src/data/database.ts:23-26`). The text promises "No se ha borrado nada" and the code keeps that promise.
- **One attempt per press.** `retry` returns early unless `statusRef.current === "failed"` and no attempt is running (`use-database-start.ts:49`); the refs make two presses in one render start one attempt; the button is disabled while `retrying`. There is no timer, interval or effect that calls it again: the only automatic call is the mount effect, and `initializeDatabase()` shares the in-flight promise, so StrictMode's double effect opens once.
- **Deep link while the message shows.** `_layout.tsx:56-59` returns `null` while opening and the message while failed, so the `Stack` (and every route) is not rendered: no route code runs and no write can happen. After a successful retry the `Stack` mounts at the linked route, which is the same read-only open as a normal launch (existing audit: routes carry only numeric ids, every write is from a press).
- **Splash and rendering.** `hideAsync()` is called once the status leaves `opening` (`_layout.tsx:52-54`), so the coach no longer sees the splash forever. This closes SEC-D2-3 (silent unusable app). The earlier claim in this file that "`_layout.tsx:26` renders nothing until the database is open" is now `_layout.tsx:56`; the guarantee is the same.

### T088 · blocked permissions

- **`app.config.js` is plain and safe.** 25 lines: it reads only `process.env.APP_VARIANT`, returns `config` untouched unless the value is exactly `"production"` (`!==` strict comparison, so `Production`, `prod` or an empty value do nothing), and then adds `android.permission.INTERNET` once. No network, no file access, no `eval`, no `require` besides the CommonJS export. Semgrep clean.
- **Generated manifest, checked.** Without `APP_VARIANT`: `INTERNET` present (no marker); `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW`, `VIBRATE`, `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` and `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` have `tools:node="remove"`. With `APP_VARIANT=production`: the same four plus `INTERNET` have `tools:node="remove"`. `android:allowBackup="false"`, `android:dataExtractionRules="@xml/data_extraction_rules"` and `android:fullBackupContent="@xml/full_backup_content"` are present in both; the XML files have 9 and 18 `domain` entries (nine domains, as the checklist requires). The four permissions are blocked for every profile.
- **Development keeps `INTERNET`.** Correct and necessary: the dev client and the debug build reach Metro through it. The generated `android/app/src/debug/AndroidManifest.xml` (Expo template) also adds `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW` and `usesCleartextTraffic="true"` for debug builds only. Not in the release variant; I did not build one to confirm.

### Findings

| ID       | Severity                               | Finding                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Concrete fix                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| -------- | -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SEC-D2-7 | Important, before the release (not D2) | The `INTERNET` block is opt-in and fails open: `app.config.js:15` blocks it only when `APP_VARIANT === "production"`. A production build made without that variable (a future `eas.json` profile that forgets `env`, a manual `eas build`, a local release) keeps `INTERNET` with no error, which breaks the control "the Internet permission is blocked in the published Android build" (principle 3). Today there is no `eas.json`, so no release can be built by mistake. | When EAS is set up: create `eas.json` with `build.production.env.APP_VARIANT = "production"` and keep the development profiles without it. Before each release, run `aapt dump permissions <apk>` (or MobSF) on the AAB/APK and require that `android.permission.INTERNET` is absent. Both are now listed in `docs/security.md`, "Before each release". Do not change the default to fail closed: it would break Metro in development. |
| SEC-D2-8 | Minor                                  | `docs/security.md` and the earlier SEC-D2-1 text still described the permissions as pending. Fixed by T088; the audit-of-each-change list had no item for `app.config.js` or for the blocked permissions.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Done: two items added to "Audit of each change" in `docs/security.md`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| SEC-D2-9 | Minor, not security                    | `DatabaseOpenFailed` sets no background or text colour (`database-open-failed.tsx`, `styles.screen` and `styles.message`), while `app.json` has `userInterfaceStyle: "automatic"`. In dark mode the message may be dark text on a dark window. Not verified (no device). It is a legibility question for the Reviewer and the device test, not a data risk.                                                                                                                  | Check the message on a device in light and dark mode; if unreadable, take the colours from the navigation theme or set both explicitly.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

SEC-D2-3 (silent splash when the database fails) is closed by T087. SEC-D2-1 stays open only for the release steps (`eas.json`, release manifest, plist, MobSF).

### Dependencies and scans

- No dependency added or changed in either commit.
- `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high`: exit 0 (only moderate development-chain findings, below the gate).
- `osv-scanner scan source --lockfile=package-lock.json`: no issues; the 10 filtered vulnerabilities are the `osv-scanner.toml` exceptions already approved on 2026-11-10 (SEC-D2-4 expiry unchanged).
- gitleaks (`git --redact`, 160 commits): no leaks. Semgrep (`p/typescript`, `p/react`, `p/secrets`, over `src`, `scripts` and `app.config.js`): no findings.

### What I could NOT verify

- **No device, simulator or emulator.** Not run: the message and the Retry button on screen, a real database failure, the deep-link behaviour on a device, dark-mode legibility (SEC-D2-9).
- **No release build.** I inspected `prebuild` output only (no Gradle build, no merged release manifest, no APK or AAB, no MobSF). So not confirmed: that the merged release manifest really drops the five permissions and that the debug overlay (`SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW`, cleartext) does not reach the release variant; that the app works without `INTERNET` on a device.
- **iOS** was not prebuilt in this pass (T088 touches Android only); the dev-client plist residue is still on the release list.

### Verdict (Security · T087 and T088)

- **Blocking:** none. **Important:** one, for the release and not for D2 (SEC-D2-7). **Minor:** two (SEC-D2-8 fixed, SEC-D2-9 for the device test).
- From a security standpoint D2 can be published (merged and used in development builds). Files changed by this audit: this section and `docs/security.md`.

## Security · Expo SDK 58 patch updates (2026-11-17)

Scope: the uncommitted change to `package.json` and `package-lock.json` on branch `fix/expo-sdk58-patches`, made with `npx expo install --fix` after `npx expo-doctor` failed on "packages match SDK versions". Fernando approved it in the chat on 2026-11-17. Method: read the `package.json` and lock diffs, compared the lock before (`HEAD`) and after (a script over both JSON files), read the `sdk-58` changelogs of the bumped packages, grepped `src/`, ran `npm audit --omit=dev`, `npm audit` and `osv-scanner scan source --lockfile=package-lock.json` as `scripts/verify.sh` runs them, and compared the audit with a copy of the `HEAD` lock. Nothing in the code was changed and no dependency was installed.

### What changed

| Package            | Before  | After   | Kind  | Changelog, in security terms                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------------ | ------- | ------- | ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `@expo/ui`         | 58.0.28 | 58.0.29 | patch | iOS `BottomSheet` text wrapping, Android `MenuView` items. UI only.                                                                                                                         |
| `expo`             | 58.0.25 | 58.0.26 | patch | iOS dev-server socket on port 443 for `https` bundle URLs (dev tunnel only); updated dependencies (`@expo/cli`, `@expo/metro-config`, `babel-preset-expo`, `expo-file-system`).             |
| `expo-dev-client`  | 58.0.20 | 58.0.21 | patch | Only "updated dependencies" (`expo-dev-launcher` 58.0.21, the same dev-server socket change). Development builds only.                                                                      |
| `expo-router`      | 58.0.31 | 58.0.32 | patch | `NativeTabs` fixes and warnings. Peer range of `react-native-screens` is now `^4.30.1 \|\| ^5.0.0-beta.1`; installed 4.30.1 still satisfies it. No route-parameter or link-handling change. |
| `expo-sqlite`      | 58.0.17 | 58.0.18 | patch | Web worker only (WebAssembly load). No native, path or backup change; `defaultDatabaseDirectory` is untouched, so the `Documents/SQLite` exclusion still holds.                             |
| `expo-web-browser` | 58.0.8  | 58.1.0  | minor | New optional HTTP headers for authentication sessions (Android, iOS 17.4+). Additive; no new permission.                                                                                    |
| `jest-expo`        | 58.0.14 | 58.0.15 | patch | Development only.                                                                                                                                                                           |

### Lock diff

- **Added or removed packages: none.** The set of lock entries is the same before and after (checked by script). 25 entries changed version: the 7 above plus their Expo companions (`expo-dev-launcher`, `expo-file-system`, `@expo/cli`, `@expo/metro-config`, `@expo/router-server`, `babel-preset-expo`) and unchanged-owner transitives: `noxcturnal` and its 8 platform binaries `0.1.12 -> 0.1.14` (pulled by `@expo/metro-config`, already there before), `ws` `8.22.2 -> 8.23.0` (Expo CLI), `postcss` `8.5.32 -> 8.5.33` and `@babel/plugin-transform-class-static-block` `7.29.7 -> 7.29.14` (plus a raised dependency range on `@babel/helper-create-class-features-plugin`, same installed version).
- **Install scripts:** no entry gained `hasInstallScript`. **Resolved URLs:** every `resolved` is `https://registry.npmjs.org/`. **Integrity:** present on every entry. **Licences:** all MIT.
- **Permissions and native config:** none of the changelogs mention a permission, a manifest or `Info.plist` key, or a config plugin option. `app.json` is not touched in the diff, so `allowBackup: false`, the blocked permissions and `./plugins/with-backup-exclusion.js` are as audited before.
- **Network:** the only network-related entry is the `expo` / `expo-dev-launcher` change for the development server socket (port 443 when the bundle URL is `https` with no port). It runs in development builds only; the production app has no dev launcher and no network use (principle 3 unchanged).

### `expo-web-browser` minor bump

- Not imported anywhere in `src/`, `tests/`, `plugins/` or `app.config.js` (grep for `expo-web-browser` and `WebBrowser`). It appears in `app.json` `plugins` (template entry, no options) and as a peer dependency of `expo-router`. The new auth-session headers are an opt-in argument of `openAuthSessionAsync`, which the app never calls. Its `package.json` has no `scripts`, and its plugin (`withWebBrowser`) adds no permission. Impact on the app: none.
- Minor point, not caused by this change: the package is in the app's dependencies only through the template and `expo-router`. Whether to drop it from `package.json` and `app.json` is a clean-up for the developer to decide; it is not a security finding.

### Scanners (as `scripts/verify.sh` runs them)

| Step                                                   | Before (`HEAD` lock)             | After (this change)                                                              |
| ------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high`              | exit 1: 68 (6 moderate, 62 high) | exit 1: 68 (6 moderate, 62 high)                                                 |
| `npm audit` (all)                                      | 94 (20 moderate, 74 high)        | 93 (19 moderate, 74 high)                                                        |
| `osv-scanner scan source --lockfile=package-lock.json` | same 2 findings (picomatch)      | exit 1: 2 High, `picomatch` 2.3.2 and 4.0.7, both GHSA-fc5q-39xc-vjpf (CVSS 8.7) |

The change adds no vulnerability and removes one (`eslint-config-expo` no longer appears in the full audit). Both gates fail because of a **new advisory that is not caused by this change and that the earlier audits did not see** (they ran `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` at exit 0 on 2026-11-14 and 2026-11-12):

- **GHSA-fc5q-39xc-vjpf**, `picomatch` "ReDoS through single-branch repeated extglobs", high, CVSS 3.1 `AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H` (denial of service only), range `<=4.0.7`, **no patched version exists** (`first_patched_version` is null; npm's suggested fix is a downgrade to `expo@52`, which is not an option). The `HEAD` lock holds the same versions (`4.0.7` at the root, `2.3.2` under `anymatch`, `jest-util` and `micromatch`), so the old lock fails in the same way. The 62 "high" entries of `npm audit --omit=dev` are this one advisory propagated up the dependency tree (`expo`, `react-native`, `expo-router`, `jest`... all "depend on vulnerable versions of picomatch" or, for `react-native` and `@react-native/*`, of `tinyglobby`, which uses it).
- **Reach:** `picomatch` is a glob matcher used by Metro, Expo CLI, Jest and `anymatch` to match file paths against our own patterns, at build, test and bundle time. It is not in the application bundle that runs on the device, and no attacker controls a pattern. The risk to the app and to the children's data is none; the exposure is a slow build or test run.
- **The other findings** (glob GHSA-3g3m-2fjr-6pw4, minimatch GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249, the four `qs` ones, esbuild GHSA-67mh-4wv8-2f99) are the existing exceptions in `osv-scanner.toml`, filtered as before (9 filtered). `GHSA-gcqx-w48m-w2h4` (deepmerge) is now reported by OSV-Scanner as an unused ignore: harmless, it can stay until its expiry or be removed.

### Findings

| ID          | Severity                                                  | Finding                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Action                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| SEC-SDK58-1 | None for the bump                                         | The seven bumps and their transitive updates: no new or removed package, no install script, registry URLs and integrity present, MIT, no permission, no network use in the app, no native config change. `expo-web-browser` is unused in `src/`.                                                                           | No action. Accept the change.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| SEC-SDK58-2 | Blocking for `scripts/verify.sh` and CI, not for the bump | The new picomatch advisory (high, no fix) makes `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` and `osv-scanner` exit 1, with or without this change, so `verify.sh` fails at "Dependencies" even when everything else is green. There is no exception yet, and `docs/security.md` and the role forbid ignoring it without one. | Needs Fernando's decision, not a silent fix. For OSV-Scanner: add `GHSA-fc5q-39xc-vjpf` to `osv-scanner.toml` with the reason above, an expiry of at most 90 days (2027-02-15) and his approval. `npm audit` has no exception file, so `scripts/verify.sh` line 84 would also need a temporary, recorded change (for example a documented `--audit-level=critical`, or an allowlist step) approved by him, and reverted when picomatch is fixed. |

### What I could NOT verify

- That the three Expo changelog summaries are complete: I read the `CHANGELOG.md` of each package on the `sdk-58` branch, not the diff of each published tarball. No tarball was unpacked beyond reading `expo-web-browser`'s `package.json` and plugin in `node_modules`.
- Runtime behaviour on a device (the dev-server socket change, `@expo/ui` pickers).
- Whether the advisory has a fix in a pre-release `picomatch`; the advisory database says none is published.

### Verdict (Security · Expo SDK 58 patch updates)

- **Blocking:** none for the dependency change itself (SEC-SDK58-1). **Blocking for the verification gate:** SEC-SDK58-2, independent of this change; the branch cannot go green until Fernando approves an exception for it. **Important / minor:** none.
- Files changed by this audit: this section and the "New dependencies" note in `plan.md`.

- Date: 2026-11-17 22:48
